Last two record fields unexplained. |
| **ZAP archive (`.zap`)** (Thrillville: Off the Rails) | LE `ZAP\0` {alignment 0x8000, count, ?}; entries `\r\n`, u32 size, u16 name len + 1, name; `dir/` opens and `..` closes a directory; data from the alignment, each member rounded up to it | → members under their paths (streamed) | extract | Implemented: all 30 retail `.zap` (up to 540 MB) validate; inner `.ovl` / `.aud` / `.prk` / `.rsf` not decoded. |
| **STA1 / STP0-STP6 stage object tables (`.bin`)** (Sonic and the Secret Rings / Black Knight) | LE {magic, object count, parameter entry count, parameter bytes}; 0x30-byte object records (float x/y/z + raw words + index), 8-byte parameter headers {kind, size}, packed parameter payloads | → `stp.txt` (objects + parameter payload bytes) | extract | Partial: all 155 sampled Secret Rings files dump; field meanings (rotation, object type ids, parameter payloads) not decoded. |
| **`.str` module path list** (Wii titles' `dll/*.str`, `main.str`, `boot.str`) | plain NUL-separated list of the build-host paths of the game's relocatable modules (`.plf` / `.rso` / `.rel`) | -- | none | Plain text; nothing to extract (nothing binary behind it). |
| **CRI AAX (`.aax`)** (Mario & Sonic London 2012) | big endian `@UTF` table "AAX", columns `data` (offset, size) / `lpflg`; each row an ADX | → `NNN.adx` (then WAV via the ADX decoder) | extract | Implemented: 3225 retail files all parse; the extracted ADX decodes through the existing ADX path. |
| **"waud" PCM sound (`.wtxs`, `.wtss`)** (Cook Wars, Cooking Party, Imagine - Fashion Idol) | 32 byte header (bytes/sample, channels, rate as u24be) + raw big endian s16 / unsigned 8 bit PCM | → `.wav` | extract | Implemented: layout derived from the data (rate / size fields match the file sizes, smooth waveforms). |
| **WTXID object image (`.wtxg`, `.wtxa`)** (Cook Wars, Cooking Party, Imagine - Fashion Idol, Petz Rescue) | magic `WTXID` + kind (g geometry / a animation), BE version 2, 0x80 byte header table; body is a serialised C++ object heap (class names `CClumpDef` / `CItemSkinMeshDef` / `CMtlStdDef`, skeleton bone and material names, BE pointers and floats) | → `<stem>.txt` string inventory; `.wtxg` meshes also `<stem>[.N].obj` + `.skin.txt` | extract | Partial: geometry decoded (length-prefixed arrays: uv f32 pairs, GX triangle display list of {u16 vertex, u16 uv} pairs, vertices {f32 pos, f32 normal, u8 n, n x {u16 bone, f32 weight}}; the 2379-vertex chef model renders correctly; skin bone indices resolve through the per-bone name list between the CClumpDef and CItemSkinMeshDef strings, e.g. vertex 0 -> `CSBip Neck1`); `.wtxa` header {frames, fps, bone count} + the same bone name list are listed, the packed key data and materials are not decoded. `.wtxa` key data (header words {frames, fps, bones}, then the length prefixed bone names, `u32 bones`, one f32 per bone (rest translation component), `u32 bones`, then bones + 1 blocks of `{u32 N, N records}`; a record is `u32 frame, f32 quat xyzw, f32 pos xyz` (8 words) or the same plus `f32 scale xyz` (11 words, chosen per block); block i = name i, the last block is an extra node; all 1003 Cook Wars clips tile their file exactly, name mapping and quaternion convention inferred) is listed as `track` lines in the `.txt`. Geometry (`.wtxg`) is also written as a static `.gltf` (position / normal / uv per corner, no skin; the skin weights stay in `.skin.txt`) Skeleton (in `.wtxg`, between the `CClumpDef` and `CItemSkinMeshDef` strings): per bone `{u32 word, u32 name length, name, f32 quat xyzw, f32 pos xyz (local rest pose)[, f32 scale xyz (~1, newer models)]}`; the u32 behind a record's floats holds in its low 16 bits the number of descendants of that bone, so the parent is the innermost earlier bone whose range covers the index. Skinned meshes are also written as `.skin.gltf` (nodes with TRS, children, one skin, inverse bind matrices from the rest pose, JOINTS_0 / WEIGHTS_0 = the four strongest weights); checked on the data: vertices dominated by a finger / lip / brow bone lie within centimetres of that bone's rest position, the rest pose is a symmetric standing figure matching the mesh bounding box, and the animation quaternions of `.wtxa` oscillate around the rest quaternions (79 of 100 meshes with a skeleton; single-bone props stay unskinned). |
| **Thrillville serialised objects (`.prk` PARK, `.tbp` TRAK, `.atr` ATTR)** (Thrillville: Off the Rails) | magic + packed token stream from the game's stream class: 7 bit ints, 30 bit floats (6 bit exponent bias 96), 12 / 20 / 28 / 32 bit ints, milli fixed point, bools, strings (last char has bit 7), 0xff end; first token = version (109 / 113) | → `<stem>.txt` token dump | extract | Partial: encoding fully decoded from the DOL encoder (0x80389c80) and verified on park saves (money, game time, ...); the field meaning is known for the start of PARK version 113 only, traced in the park writer `FUN_8037f1fc` (every writer call is one token): token 1 LevelNumber, 2-4 GameTime (3 floats), 5-146 Money (3 ints, a float 0.01, a byte, 4 x 10 ints, 2 ints, 5 graphed values of 16 floats + 2 bytes + bool), 147-155 Marketing, 156-168 Research, 169 the attraction count followed by the entries (skip count + 0xf0 byte object each); the `.txt` gets a `# fields:` line for PARK 113. ATTR is one attraction entry (the writer `FUN_8032a874`, also used for the park's attraction list): after the version a 4 bit kind (6 = ride with a track, 8 = minigolf grid, 7 = ...), 8 small bit fields, a vector of 4 floats, 3 more bit fields, kind 8: grid width and height + width x height bytes + 3 floats, a counted byte list, 10 bit fields, 9 floats, a counted signed byte list, the name as 32 x u16 character codes, 3 x u16, a 32 bit flag word and a flag; the two version 112 minigolf ATTR files match this to the token, version 108 files lack one token, and kind 6 embeds the TRAK writer (`FUN_80214c00`, a recursive node tree: not mapped); the `.txt` gets a `# fields:` line for version 112 kind 8. The other PARK objects are only known from the writers, not located in the stream (they follow the attraction list, whose kind 6 entries embed the undecoded track blob): a group entry (`FUN_80394940`) is 15 tokens {4, 10 bit, 1 bit, 2 floats, s16, float, 4 bit, u16, float, 3 flags, 2 x 10 bit ints}, a staff entry (`FUN_803acac4`) 12 tokens {int, float, 2 bit, 1 bit, 4 floats, float, 7 bit, 2 flags}, a guest entry (`FUN_80399e70`) starts with an int and 9 floats and ends with 10 bit, 1, 1, 7 bit, 1, 1 around two sub-records; arrays are written as a count token, then per element a skip count token and the entry, then a closing block. TRAK is not annotated. Text stored as arrays of character codes (e.g. `%PreBuiltOFFTHERAILS`, a prebuilt ride name in the park saves) is recovered and listed as `# token N: "text"` lines; characters above 63 take the 12 bit token form. |
| **Sega-style PVR (`XIBG`/`TRVP`) texture** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | *Radirgy Noa* and *Illvelo* `.pvr`: BE `XIBG` (GBIX) + `TRVP` (PVRT) header, `type = kind << 8 | pixel format`, width/height BE at `+0x1c`. Kind `0x45` = raw 4x4-tiled BE 16-bit texels (pixel format 0 = ARGB1555, 1 = RGB565, 2 = ARGB4444), `0x4e` = GX CMPR; both decode to `<file>.png` (checked on the CRIWARE logo, font and stage atlases); kind `0x48` (`toon.pvr`, 256x256) = 16-entry ARGB1555 palette at `0x20` + 4-bit indices in 8x8 tiles (high nibble first), also decoded; `tests/mk_ggxarchiver.py` (PASS) |
| **Kylotonn FVRT archive** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *My Body Coach* (`BodyCoaching.dat`, 1,151 members, 490 MB), *My Body Coach 2*, *Cocoto Festival* (`cocoto.dat`): `FVRT`, hash, `VERS`, `FILE` + u32 LE payload size (members stored back to back from `0x1c`), then a `SARR` index (chunks are `{tag, u32 LE size}`): `TEXT` holds `{count, 0}` and `count` x 32-byte `{-1, -1, hash, -1, flags, size, offset, name offset}` entries, `TBUF` is the NUL separated path pool (entry *i* has name *i*). Members are streamed to `<file>.d/<path>` (`.vid` movies, `.snd`, `.anm`, `.mdl`, `.txc`, ... are not decoded further); `tests/mk_kylotonn.py` (PASS) |
| **Ubisoft Imagine WIZ model** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | *Beastly* / *Babyz*-engine `.wiz` static models (74 of 93 *Beastly* files; skinned and large variants are skipped), after the LZSS wrapper: big-endian `{u32 mesh blob size, u32 mesh table offset}`, mesh table `{count, record offsets}`; each record has relative offsets to position (float3), colour (u16) and texcoord (float2) arrays (each preceded by its byte size), vertex modes at `+0x2c` (2 = u8 index), the position count (u16) at `+0x3a`, and a GX display list at `+0x80` (0x80 quads / 0x90 triangles / 0x98 strip / 0xa0 fan, one index per attribute). The texture table after the blob gives `{data offset, u16 w, u16 h, GX format}` (CMPR 1024x1024 atlases decode cleanly). Level and skinned files (69 of the 71 distinct *Beastly* `.wiz`; `ciel` and `particule` are a different `.wiz` kind: their header is `{0x34, 0, ...}` with no mesh table, probably sky / particle data, not decoded) add a normal array at `+4` and keep the attribute modes in GX descriptor order at `+0x2c` (position), `+0x2d` (normal), `+0x2e` (colour 0), `+0x30` (texcoord 0), 0 = absent, 2 / 3 = 8 / 16 bit index; a record can hold several display-list segments (after the first at `+0x80`, each on a 0x20 boundary, separated by other data) up to the next record, records without a display list (empty or other data) are skipped and counted in the log (checked: median triangle edge a few dozen units on a 12000 unit level, after an earlier index-size ambiguity gave 1350). Written as `model.obj` (with `model.mtl` when the file has a single texture) plus `tex_NNNN.png`; the normals themselves, bone weights / skinning and material-to-texture mapping are not decoded; `tests/mk_imaginewiz.py` (PASS) |
| **Ubisoft Imagine LOC (`.LOCV1.0`)** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | *Beastly*, *Babysitting Party*, *Babyz* `.LOC` files (LZSS wrapped, see above): `.LOCV1.0`, `u32 id`, 4-byte language tag, then LE `u32` string offsets from `0x10` to the first string, then NUL-terminated Latin-1 texts. Exported to `strings.tsv` (`index<TAB>text`, UTF-8, `\n \r \t \\` escaped; a few leading entries are empty); verified on *Beastly* `odyssey_DE.LOC` (850 German strings); `tests/mk_loc.py` (PASS) |
| **Ubisoft Imagine WSP sprite** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | The `.wsp` files of *Babysitting Party* / *Babyz* engine games after the LZSS wrapper: LE `{u32 GX format (4 RGB565, 5 RGB5A3, 6 RGBA8), u32 width, u32 height}`, `0xcc` padding to `0x20`, then GX tiles for width and height rounded up to 4 (853x480 is stored 856x480); decoded to PNG (checked on the tutorial backgrounds); `tests/mk_imaginelz.py` (PASS) |
| **Ubisoft Imagine MSK / TAN** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | After the LZSS wrapper: `.msk` = LE `{u32 width, u32 height}`, 16 x `0xcc`, `width*height` 8-bit mask bytes (greyscale PNG; 144 of 146 files in *Babysitting Party*); `.tan` = BE `{u32 frames, u32 width, u32 height, 0}`, a 0x210-byte RGB565 palette, then `frames` GX C8 images (8x4 tiles) of `width*height` bytes, written as `frame_NN.png` (250/250 face/mouth animation files decode); `tests/mk_imaginelz.py` (PASS) |
| **International Athletics TEX** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | `.TEX` members of `GameData.pak`: BE header `{0x0402xxxx, u32 format, u32 data size, u32 width, u32 height, 1}`, data at `+0x18`, the file ends exactly after it. Format 9 = C4 and 10 = C8 (RGB5A3 palette first, then GX tiles), 11 = RGB5A3, 13 = CMPR, rows stored bottom-up; all 511 textures of the game decode to PNG; `tests/mk_pak3.py` covers a C4 texture (PASS) |
| **Telltale ttarch v3-6** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Classic Telltale archives of *Sam & Max: Beyond Time and Space* (Wii, 90/90 retail `.ttarch`: 8,912 members in one `data` archive, `ERTM` resources): LE header `{version 4, info_mode 0, type3, files_mode, chunk count, chunk sizes, data size, 2 dummies, info size}`, unencrypted info table, then stored data (mode 1) or 64 KiB **zlib-wrapped** chunks forming one stream the member offsets point into (mode 2; v8/9 chunks are raw deflate, handled by the same `lib-ttarch.c`). Layout per Luigi Auriemma's `ttarchext`; `tests/mk_ttarch4.py` (PASS). Blowfish-encrypted archives (info_mode 1) and v7 are not handled |
| **Natsume add PAC / LZS** | Wii / Archive + Compression | ✅ | ❌ | *Gintama: Yorozuya Chuubu - Tsukkomable Douga* `.pac` (1,438 files): `add\0` header (table offset `+8`, count `+0x10`), 0x20-byte entries `{offset, size, 0, timestamp, name offset}` (BE on Wii, LE accepted); members named `*z` are `LZS\0` streams (u8 length bits `+5`, unit bits `+6` = 8/16, flag stream `+8`, literal pool `+0xc`, size `+0x10`, threshold `+0x19`; 16-bit flag words, bit 0 first, 1 = literal unit; match word = distance << bits \| (length - threshold), in units of 1 or 2 bytes). Decoded members (`brres`, `brtex`, `anm`) extract further; layout and algorithm cross-checked against the QuickBMS `add_pac`/`lzs` notes and Nisto's `lzsd`; 205/205 sampled retail archives, `tests/mk_addpac.py` (PASS) |
| **Ubisoft BIGFILE TOC/DAT + DKAP** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Totally Spies! Totally Party* `vdb_NGC/BIGFILEn.TOC` + `.DAT`: head `{count, 1, type hash, size}` padded to 0x24, then 36-byte records `{group, id, type hash, size, offset x4, flag}`; one member is the id -> `D:\TSPIES\PROD\GAME\VDB_REPOSITORY\...` path table (`{id, len, path, NUL}` 4-byte aligned) so all 2,796 members get their real names. `DKAP` members (`"DKAP"`, u32 LE unpacked size, LZO1X stream at +8, routine recovered from the DOL with Ghidra) are unpacked (1,167 bitmaps, 624 `NVMESH`, 342 `NVSKELANIM`, 340 `NVSKELETON`, 15 `AMSH`); `_NGCSD02` streams are kept as `.ngcsd`; `tests/mk_dkapbig.py` (PASS) |
| **NGCBM (`_NGCBM01`)** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | 0x20-byte header `{"_NGCBM01", u16 w, u16 h, u16 GX format (6 RGBA8, 14 CMPR seen), u8 mips, u8 flags, 0, u32 data offset}` + GX texels; decoded to PNG by wrapping the first level in a TPL; all 1,167 *Totally Spies! Totally Party* bitmaps decode |
| **NGCSD (`_NGCSD02`)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `"_NGCSD02"`, f32 duration, u32 samples, flag byte at `+0x12` (0 mono, 1 mono + trailing data, 2 stereo + trailing data), one standard 0x60-byte DSP header per channel from `+0x18`, then ADPCM frames interleaved one 8-byte frame per channel (stereo L/R correlation 0.81 for 8-byte, ~0 for half/larger splits); each channel written as `audio.dsp` / `chN.dsp` (and WAV); all 295 *Totally Spies! Totally Party* streams validate (ADPCM frame headers 100% plausible, each channel's first byte equals its header `ps`); `tests/mk_ngcsd.py` (PASS) |
| **FBND bundle** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Sega Bass Fishing* / *Uchi Tsuri!* `data.bin` (`"FBND"`, u32 0x630, file table zeroed in retail): members sit at 0x800-aligned offsets and are carved by streaming (the file exceeds the in-memory limit): U8 archives (size = end of the last file node) become `NNNNN_<offset>.arc` and are extracted recursively; `BCV0` resources (`BCV0`/`STRS`/`KIND`/`CROW`/`EOCK` name index plus raw payload up to the next member) become `.bcv`. The `.dat` is carved by streaming into `NNNNN.bin` and the members are extracted recursively (U8 archives; the rest are `HXTB`, `FBTI`, `HXCB`, `HXAA` data). |

Written as `<name>.json`. All 157 files parse to exactly EOF; in the 94 non-empty ones the frame count equals the header total and every rectangle lies inside `MAXB`. |
| **KSS Wave Link Data** | Wii / Sound bank | ✅ | ❌ | *MLB Power Pros* `sound/WaveData.bin` ("KSS Wave Link Data", Konami sound system; layout reverse engineered from the retail file, no public spec): 5 sample banks, each a region table (12-byte entries with the sample start), a table of 0x40-byte descriptors that are the DSP header from offset 0x0c on (loop flag, loop start, end, coefficients, history) and a DSP-ADPCM data blob. 302 samples are written as standard `.dsp` (and decode to plausible audio: loud, no ADPCM blow-up). The sample rate is not stored in any identified field, so 32000 Hz is written (may be wrong). `strbgm.bin` and `voice*.bin` are the Konami `.vas` container (previous rows); the link tables `KSS SE Link Data` (`SeData.bin`) and `KSS BGM Link Data` (`TrSeData.bin`) are not decoded. |

Written as `<file>.png`; all 842 files of the Spanish disc are recognised (the blue/red order was judged from skin textures).

| **Sonic Team ONE `.onz` / `.one`** | Wii / Container | ✅ | ❌ | *Sonic Unleashed* `one_wii*/**/*.onz`: the whole file is one LZ11 stream; the data is `{"one.", u8 count, 3 zero bytes}` and 0x40-byte records `{name[0x38], u32 LE offset, u32 LE size}` (nested `.one` archives are the same layout uncompressed). Members (GVR textures, `gncp`/`gno`/`gnm` scene data, `nut`) are written by name (`~<n>` for repeated names) and recursed into; GVR decode to PNG. All 621 sampled files (gallery, load_etc, a stage) extract. |
| **Marvelous `MSGP` `.mpc`** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | *Harvest Moon: Animal Parade* `msglang/<lang>/<sex>/*.mpc`: `{"MSGP", 0x01000001, count, 0x14, data offset}`, `{id, offset, size}` entries and one `MSGB` block per entry (four small fields + UTF-16BE text with `%k%`-style control codes). Written as `<file>.tsv` (id, a, b, c, d, text). All 546 files on the disc are recognised. |
| **`ALIG` `.aig` textures** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | *One Piece: Unlimited Adventure* `material/*.aig`: 0x40-byte header `{"ALIG", 0x0102, format tag (GIA4 / G5A3 / G565 / GC32 / GCCP = IA4 / RGB5A3 / RGB565 / RGBA8 / CMPR), LE width, LE height, ..., BE level-0 size}` + GX tiled data; level 0 is decoded to PNG (all 22 files). |
| **CRI Atom cue-sheet bank `.csb`** | Wii / Audio bank | ✅ | ❌ | *Pandora's Tower* / *Ghost Squad* `*.csb` (`@UTF` table `TBLCSB` with nested `CUE`, `SYNTH`, `SOUND_ELEMENT`, `ISAAC` tables; not to be confused with the Paper Mario collision `.csb`). Every `SOUND_ELEMENT` row holds an `ADPCM_WII` table whose `header` (0x60 byte DSP header) + `data` blobs are written as `<wave name>.dsp` and decode to WAV (125 of the 126 Pandora's Tower files; the remaining one is a streaming bank without embedded audio). Cue / synth parameter tables are not decoded. |
| **Sega Ninja-GC model `.gno`** | Wii / 3D model | ✅ | ❌ | *Super Monkey Ball: Banana Blitz* / *Ghost Squad* `*.gno` (`NGIF`/`NGTL`/`NGOB`/`NOF0`/`NFN0`/`NEND` chunks, pointers relative to 0x20, listed in `NOF0`). The GX display lists (quads / triangles / strips / fans with INDEX16 position, normal, texcoord) are resolved against the float3 / s8 / s16 vertex arrays and written as `model.obj`, one object per display list. Node transforms, skinning and materials are not applied; the sibling `.gnm/.gnj/.gnt/.gnv/.gnf/.gnh/.gnd` files are accepted but only the model files are known to carry geometry. |
| **Procyon Studio sound bank `.swd`** | Wii / Audio bank | ✅ | ❌ | *Arc Rise Fantasia* `*.SWD` (`swdb`, big-endian; chunks `wavi`, `prgi`, `kgrp`, `pcmd`, `eod `). The `pcmd` chunk holds complete DSP-ADPCM sounds (0x60 byte header + data) back to back; each is written as `<n>.dsp` and decodes to WAV. Program / keygroup / `.sed` sequence data are not decoded. The companion `.vld` files are plain dialogue text records (`{u32 id, u32 size, ...}` + NUL terminated English text). |
| **Konami `.afs` container** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Pro Evolution Soccer 2012* `boot.afs`, `over.afs`, `*_sound.afs`, `*_text.afs` (not the CRI `AFS\0` archive): `{u8 4, 3 flag bytes, u32 BE size, ... u32 BE count at 0x20, count + 1 u32 BE offsets relative to 0x20}`. Members are stored (`SDHS`, `PACS`, `INFO` ...) or wrapped in a 0x20 byte header (packed / unpacked size) followed by a zlib stream, which is inflated. Written as `<n>.<tag>` (`.bin` for unknown payloads) and extracted recursively; the sound / text archives (200-750 MB) are streamed member by member. Only `boot.afs` has been tested. |
| **Race-O-Rama `.gcn.res` resources** | Wii / Resource bundle | ✅ | ❌ | *Cars: Race-O-Rama* `Res/Data/*/*.gcn.res` (little-endian head `{3, 0x1c, head size, ...}` with the original resource path, zlib streams from 0x800 separated by single `0xff` pad bytes). The inflated payload is concatenated; textures (BE head `{2, flags, 0, 1, w, h, w, h, size}` + CMPR data) are written as PNG, every other resource as `<name>.bin`. Only the loading-screen texture has been verified; other resource kinds (models, animation, UI) are inflated but not parsed. |
| **Agatha Christie `.wii` bundle** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Agatha Christie: And Then There Were None* `*.wii` (e.g. `dialogue.wii`; unrelated to the Ubisoft `.wii` heap images): LE `u32 count`, 0x1c zero bytes, `count` 0x100-byte records `{path[0xf4] (backslashes), u32 offset, u32 size, u32 used}`, files back to back. Extracted below `<file>.d/` under the stored path. Only `dialogue.wii` (428 tab separated dialogue tables) has been tested. |
| **Chunked-LZF object files (`.et` `.map` `.rgn` `.loc` `.cin` ...)** | Wii / Compressed container | ✅ | ❌ | *Despicable Me: The Game* (hash-named `EntityTemplates/*.et`, `Maps/*.map`, `Regions/*.rgn`, `Regions/<lang>/*.loc`, `Cinematics/*.cin`, ...; the same files are stored uncompressed in *Alien Syndrome*). 8 byte type hash, BE unpacked size, BE offset count `n`, `n` offsets (bit 31 = chunk stored as is, last = file size), then 0x8000-byte liblzf chunks. Unpacked to `<file>.d/<file>.faaf`; the resulting object container (`FA AF FA AF`, version 3 / 7, `BBBBBBBB`-tagged blocks, `FE EF FE EF`) is not parsed. Verified on 5 files (sizes match the header). |
| **Table Tennis string table `.strtbl`** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | *Rockstar Games Presents Table Tennis* `pong/assets/strtbl/<region>/*.strtbl` (little-endian: block offsets, a key block, then one `{hash, font tag, UTF-16LE text}` block per language). Written as `<file>.tsv` (language, index, hash, font, text). The key names are validated but not joined to the texts (the entries are sorted by an unidentified key hash). All 5 language blocks of `global.strtbl` parse to the byte. |
| **Gamebryo 20.3.0.9 `.nif` (classic geometry)** | Wii / 3D model | ✅ | ❌ | *Monster Mayhem: Build and Battle*, *Dance on Broadway* `*.nif` (big-endian, `NiNode` / `NiTriStrips` / `NiTriShape` + `NiTriStripsData` / `NiTriShapeData`; the 20.6.0.0 `NiMesh` files of Pocoyo Racing are handled by the older NIF extractor). Written as `<file>.obj` in world space (node transforms applied, one object per shape, UVs included); skinning, materials and the external `.tga` textures are not applied, `.kf` / `.kfm` animation files are not decoded. Verified on Monster Mayhem's `gruffin.nif` (7 shapes, 3,556 triangles, plausible humanoid proportions). |
| **NFF bitmap font `.nff`** | Wii / Font | ✅ | ❌ | *Monster Mayhem* `font/*.nff` (`NFF\0`, version 1-3; point size, glyph code list, cell table, then a width x height RGBA8 atlas with the glyph coverage in alpha). | **Konami `PACS` sound pack (`.sep`)** | Wii / Audio bank | ✅ | ❌ | *Mahjong Fight Club Wii* `sound/*.sep` and the `SND_DATA` packs inside *Pro Evolution Soccer* `.afs` members (`PACS` + `PHED` / `PRAM` / `TONE` / `SEQ` / `BANK` / `WHED` / `WVHD` / `WDAT` chunks). | **Mahjong Fight Club `.kwtp` texture pack** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | *Mahjong Fight Club Wii* `*.kwtp` (`kwtp02`, BE head with a 0x18 byte sprite record table, TPL at the offset stored at 0x18). The sprite rectangle table and the `.kwbf` / `.kmsg` companions are not decoded. |
| **Rubik's Puzzle World `.etx` texture** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | *Rubik's Puzzle World* `textures/<hash>.etx` (BE `{u16 w, u16 h, u32 fmt<<16}` with 0x10 = GX RGBA8, 0x12 = RGB565, 0x15 = I8, GX tiled data, 24 zero trailer bytes). | **Rubik's Puzzle World `.ma` archive** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Rubik's Puzzle World* `archives/*.ma` (LE `u32 count`, `{u16 name length incl. | **`.mus` DSP-ADPCM chunks (Build-A-Bear / Alien Syndrome / Despicable Me engine)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `Sounds/<lang>/<hash>.mus` starting `EF EF EF EF` (16 coefficients, predictor / scale, histories, u32 data size at 0x2c, DSP frames from 0x30). | **Squeeballs Party `.ast` (PCM16)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | *Squeeballs Party* `Music/*.ast` (BE `{u32 frames, u16 channels, u16 rate, ...}` padded to 0x68, then interleaved PCM16; not the Nintendo `STRM` AST). | **Chicken Little `.dct` string dictionary** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | *Disney's Chicken Little: Ace in Action* `data/lang/*.dct` (LE `DICT`, version 0x1000, count at 0x10, `{hash, offset}` table, UTF-8 strings at `0x19 + 8 n + offset`). | **CRI `.awb` streaming archive (CPK with ITOC only)** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Power Rangers Samurai* `Sound/BGM_streamfiles.awb` (`CPK ` packet + `ITOC`: nested `CpkItocL` / `CpkItocH` tables of `{ID, FileSize, ExtractSize}`, members in ascending ID order from `ContentOffset`, padded to `Align`). | **CRI HCA audio `.hca`** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `.hca` files (ADX2 `.awb` / `.cpk` members, *Power Rangers Samurai*). | **Necro-Nesia `.bmsg` message table** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | `<LANG>/*.bmsg` (40 census files): LE `{1, string section offset, 0, pair count}` + 8-byte label records, section `{1, count, offsets}` + NUL-terminated UTF-16LE strings (control codes kept as `\uXXXX`). Written as `<file>.tsv` (index, text); verified on `FR/ITEM_TEXT.bmsg` (140 strings; this file holds item-name labels). The label table is not decoded. |
| **Earth Seeker `.ffx` (SYSM messages)** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | `FFX/*.ffx` (110 census files): BE `SYSM` header, table of 2×pairs pool offsets (label / message), NUL-terminated Shift-JIS text with `^t[..]` / `^c[..]` tags. Written as `<file>.tsv` (label, text); verified on `etcmessage.ffx` (513 messages of Japanese event dialogue). |
| **Earth Seeker `.pix` texture wrapper** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | `2D/*.pix` (65 census files): `FPIX` / `DPIX` / `XPIX` chunk headers in front of a complete TPL that runs to the end of the file. The TPL is written as `<file>.tpl` and decoded to PNG (verified: `battlemenu.pix` renders the battle UI atlas). `.rsam` (sound label table text), `.fon` (`FFON` font), `.divc` / `.binnavi` are not decoded.; edited PNG re-injected on repack (header kept, mip chain regenerated)|
| **Pipeworks PWK `.pvd` / `.mcl` strings** | Wii / Script | ✅ | ❌ | *Monster High: Ghoul Spirit* `Scripts/*.pvd` (script debug symbols: source names, `Class::method`, locals) and `Scripts/Scripts.mcl` (master class list, plus `.pvm` module names). The `PWK <kind> vX.YY (big endian)   \x1a` header is followed by BE offset fields and a string area; the identifier strings are written as `<file>.txt` (`offset<TAB>string`). The `.pvm` bytecode and the class records are not decoded (the first class name of the list is missed by the string scan). |
| **Capcom `.apx` images** | Wii / Texture | ◐ | ◐ | *Zack & Wiki* `Demo/Logo/Title_*.apx`: LE `{file size, pixel bytes, palette bytes, bpp, width, height, 1}` + linear RGB; the four 24-bit title screens decode to PNG. The 4 / 8 bit logo and system-font files carry a palette block whose layout is not understood and are skipped. |
| **Sting `GAME.PAH` / `GAME.PAC`** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Dokapon Kingdom*: little-endian index `{count, 27 section offsets}` + 16-byte entries `{PAC offset, size, 0, name offset}` and the 438 MB `GAME.PAC` next to it. 2,643 members (`.IGB` models, `.PIM` textures which decode to PNG, `.PIL`, `.TGA`, `.DAT`) are streamed out under their names; `.IGB` / `.PIL` are not decoded. |
| **`gamedata.fat` + `gamedata`** | Wii / Archive | ◐ | ❌ | *Battle of Giants: Dinosaurs Strike*: big-endian `{count, count * {id, 0, offset}}` index and the 85 MB `gamedata` of back-to-back zlib streams (all 4,515 inflate). Members are written decoded as `<id>.res`; each starts `{kind, size, type hash, id}` and its typed payload (e.g. hash `24097ed9` 1,720×, `39734df3` ~800×) is not decoded. The game's `.bink` movies (video) are not touched. |
| **Mario Sports Mix `stage/*.bin`** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Mario Sports Mix* `stage/s<NN><sport>.bin` (`s01BA`, `s31HO`, `s34VO`, ...): LZH8 (header byte `0x40`, 24-bit little-endian size) around a BRRES, so `wszst xx` unpacks them and exports the NW4R models to GLB. Checked on five retail files (`s01BA`, `s01DO`, `s12DO`, `s31HO`, `s34VO`): each decodes to a `bres` whose own length field equals the decoded size, byte-identical to an independent decoder (Treeki's `LHDecompressor`, which names the same two Huffman trees and LZ copy). The census listed these as unsupported only because it predates the LZH8 route. |
| **vaps page sets** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Disney-Pixar Toy Story Mania!* (341 `.wii` files), *Disney Princess: Enchanted Journey*, *George of the Jungle*, *Trivial Pursuit: Bet You Know It*: big-endian `"vaps"` image with header words `[1] 0x00010003`, `[2]` entry count, `[7]` data base (0x800 or 0x2000), `[10]`/`[11]` size/offset of a small bucket array; the `{u32 hash, u32 size, u32 offset}` entries start right after that array, offsets are relative to the data base, members sit on 0x800 boundaries and never overlap. Members have no names or type tags (about a fifth start with a `00010000` header carrying a class code such as `58008910`/`58008911`/`58008912`, the rest are raw data), so they are written flat as `<hash>.<class>.bin` / `<hash>.bin` without recursing into them. Checked on all 304 non-empty files of Toy Story Mania (every entry byte-identical to its source range; the other 37 files are empty 2 KB shells). The contents (GX textures, vertex data, object records) are not decoded. |
| **X-Men: Destiny `.zzz` chunk streams** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *X-Men: Destiny* `*.zzz` (529 of 551): big-endian concatenation of chunks `{F0 0D CA FE 12 34 43 21, u32 payload size, u32 class hash, 36 more header bytes, payload}`, a 52 byte header then the payload, the last chunk ending exactly at EOF (64,451 chunks, none left over). Chunks are written as `<index>.<class hash>.bin` including their header, except two classes whose payload is a known file and is written alone: `544872c7` (DDS textures, 173, or Scaleform GFX movies, 41; `.dds` / `.gfx`) and `0e819532` (Wwise `BKHD` sound banks, 98; `.bnk`); `wszst xx` on the folder then converts the textures to PNG and unpacks the banks; the GFX movies are already the plain uncompressed form (`GFX` magic, not the zlib `CFX` one the existing GFx extractor unpacks), so nothing further happens to them. Checked on all 529 files: every member equals its chunk or, for those two classes, its payload. The other 192 class hashes (194 in all; the biggest, `bb09d631`, is 200 MB of payload) and the payloads are not decoded. Findings: chunks of one asset share the name hash at header offset 24 and the word at offset 36 is a per-class type (0x4e data buffers, 0x45 vertex buffers with 16 byte alignment, 0x15 / 0x46 / 0x6 other resources, 0x5 small descriptors); the commonest mesh-like asset is `3cafb711` (descriptor) + `78bd18b4` (vertex attribute arrays) + `fdd6f811` (all 2,555 payloads start with `0x90`, a GameCube/Wii GX `DRAW_TRIANGLES` display list that indexes the arrays). Turning those into meshes would need the descriptor layout (vertex format and array strides), which is not known. The other 22 `.zzz` files in the game are plain text and are not touched. |
| **Scaleform GFx movie listing (plain `GFX`)** | Wii / UI movie | ◐ | ❌ | Uncompressed Scaleform GFx / SWF movies (`GFX` or `FWS` magic, as in the 41 UI movies inside *X-Men: Destiny* `.zzz` files; the zlib `CFX` form is unpacked by the GFx extractor above, whose output is then listed too): `u8[3] magic, u8 version, u32 LE size, RECT, u16 fps (8.8), u16 frames`, then SWF tags up to End. Only a `<file>.txt` listing is written: header, the top level tags by type with counts and sizes, `ExportAssets` / `ImportAssets2` names, the `ExporterInfo` movie name and the Scaleform `DefineExternalImage2` (1009) records `{u16 id, u16 0, u16 format, u16 width, u16 height, name, file name}`, which give the external DDS texture names and sizes (e.g. `Button9_img.dds`, 32x32). Vector shapes, fonts and ActionScript are not decoded. All 41 listings agree with an independent Python parser; `FWS` input was not tested. |
| **Valhalla Knights `.bxml`** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | *Valhalla Knights: Eldar Saga* `*.bxml` (1,326 of 1,327): big-endian binary XML, `u16 version, u16 0x10 (0x210 in the localised ENGLISH files), u16 flags, u16 elements N, u16 attribute entries M, 0, 0, u16 pool size`, element records, attribute entries and a NUL terminated string pool, each element record starting `{u16 first child, u16 child count, u16 first attribute, u16 attribute count}` (children are consecutive records). Version 3 (flags `0x0804`, 1,150 files): 8 byte records and 4 byte `{u16 name, u16 value}` entries, the entry at the first attribute index carries the element name and text (`0xFFFF` = none). Version 2 (`0x0c04`, 138 files): 12 byte records with `u16 name, u16 text` appended. Version 1 (`0x1008`, 38 files): 16 byte records with `u32 name, u32 text` and 8 byte `{u32 name, u32 value}` entries. Written as `<file>.xml` (Shift-JIS in the five files whose pool is not UTF-8; source comments survive as text and are written back as comments). All 1,326 parse into well-formed XML and the 1,150 version-3 files agree with an independent Python decoder; `enparam.bxml` (flags `0x0808`, header size `0x110`) is not handled: after the usual records it holds tables of `{length, offset}` pairs into a roughly 100 KB high-entropy payload (entropy 7.8 bits per byte, not zlib or raw deflate) that is not decoded. The *Big League Sports* `.bxml` files are a different format (see the next row). |
| **Big League Sports `.bxml`** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | *Big League Sports* `*.bxml` (422 files; the 439 `.bxml` files of *World Championship Sports: Summer* use the same format and all convert): big-endian binary XML, `u32 1, u32 string table size S, u32 elements N, u32 attributes M`, the string table, `N` 16 byte element records and `M` 12 byte attribute records (file size = 16 + S + 16N + 12M). Table entries are `{u32 hash, NUL terminated string}` padded so the string takes `((len + 1) & ~3) + 4` bytes. Every pointer is a signed offset relative to the word that holds it: element `{name -> entry, next sibling, first child, first attribute}` (the last three relative to the record, 0 = none), attribute `{name -> entry, value -> string, next attribute}`. The first record and its siblings are the top level elements (files starting with an `<Include>` have several, so the output is then a fragment). Written as `<file>.xml`. All 422 files decode, cover every record exactly once and agree with an independent Python decoder (the World Championship Sports files were only run through the extractor, which validates the same coverage); the name hashes are not used. |
| **Voxler `.vxla` karaoke annotations** | Wii / Karaoke | ✅ | ❌ | *Let's Sing*, *The Voice*, *Sing 4*, *Everyone Sing* `*.vxla` (about 100 games): plain ISO-8859-1 XML, `<AnnotationFile><IntervalLayer name="..."><Interval t1 t2 value/>`. The `lyrics_cut` layer holds one syllable per interval and `notes` (UINT8) the pitch for the same interval, `pages` the display pages (also `lyrics`, `notes_golden`, `preview`, `scale`, `nothing`, `no_lyrics`, and `*2` layers in duets). Exported as an UltraStar song text `<file>.txt` (`#BPM:1200`, i.e. 80 beats per second, `: start length pitch syllable`, a `- beat` line at each page change; no audio tag, pitch not transposed, golden notes and second singer layers not exported, syllables without a note of identical timing left out). 142 of 144 sampled files convert and agree with an independent Python implementation; the two 645 byte `Fake.vxla` stubs have no notes. Not tried in an UltraStar player. |
| **Voxler `.mesh.bin`** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | *Let's Sing*, *The Voice* `common/misc/*.mesh.bin` (UI quads: `Fond`, `fondatlas`, `mask`, `light1-3`, `particule1-4`), little endian: `{u8 4, "MESH"}`, `{u8 4, "VTEX", u32 count, count * 3 floats}`, optionally `{u8 4, "UV00", u32 count, count * 2 floats}`, ending exactly at EOF; no index list, the vertices are consecutive triangles. Written as Wavefront OBJ `<file>.obj` (`vt` when the counts match, faces when the vertex count is a multiple of 3). All 10 sampled files are 143 byte quads (6 vertices, 2 triangles) and convert; other tags, if any exist in other games, are not handled and make the file be skipped. |
| **BCAB collision chunk list** | Wii / Collision data | ✅ | ❌ | *Paws & Claws - Pet Resort* `TPWiiProj/COL/merged_cs.bin`, `merged_dirt.bin`, `merged_hoofs.bin`: chain of `{char name[4], u32 body size, body}` chunks (`DOGA`, `CATA`, `DRTA`, `H_HL`, ...) ending exactly at EOF; each body is `BCAB` + 10 floats (the odd `DRTC` chunk has 11 words that do not look like floats). Written as `<file>.json` with the values unnamed. `TPWiiProj/COL/Merged.bin` in the same game is an `ENV0` file (already supported). |
| **BAAO attack attribute tables** | Wii / Battle data | ✅ | ❌ | *One Piece Unlimited Adventure / Cruise* `battle/atk_attr_db.bin`, `com_atk_attr.bin`, `item_atk_attr.bin` (`BAAO`, big-endian `{tag, group count, version 1/2}` then `{n, ids offset, records offset}` per group; each group is `n` u32 ids plus `n` offsets to variable length records that run to the next offset). Verified on all three files (13, 18 and 26 groups). Written as `<file>.json` with the record words unnamed (floats where the value looks like one). |
| **VT2009 animation tables** | Wii / Animation data | ✅ | ❌ | *Virtua Tennis 2009* `Players/<ID>/rundata.dat` (56-byte records) and `swingdata.dat` (256-byte records): headerless tables whose records start with a 32-byte NUL padded animation name (`MYA_TP_F3_H1_SIM`, `ARI_STAND_LOOP_SIM`, ...) followed by a hash and unnamed ints/floats, ending with an `end` record (67 / 414 records for `ANC`). Recognised by the record pattern on `.dat`; verified on all 52 player files. Written as `<file>.json`. `servedata.dat`, `ai_data.dat` and `player_data.dat` are not covered. |
| **OMOM quest archive** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Monster Hunter 3 tri-* (JP) `quest/quest00.bin`, `quest01.bin`, `quest03.bin` (`OMOM`, big-endian `{tag, count, count * {offset, size}}`; 54 / 98 / 10 members at 0x40-aligned offsets, all inside the file). Unnamed members are written as `NNN.bin` into `<file>.d/` and extracted recursively; the members are quest records in a custom text encoding and are not decoded further. |
| **ADEV adventure events** | Wii / Script data | ✅ | ❌ | *Soul Eater: Monotone Princess* `event/AdvEvent*.bin` and `AdvRandomEvent*.bin` (`ADEV`, big-endian `{tag, header size = 16 + 8 * groups, 8 * events, group count, group first-event indices, group event counts}` then events of 40 records x 32 u16 words (`0xffff` unused); the file size equals header + events * 0xA00 exactly in all 12 files). Written as `<file>.json` with the words unnamed. |
| **v1b arrangement tables** | Wii / Layout data | ✅ | ❌ | *Nights: Journey of Dreams* `arrangement/*.bin` (`v1b\0`, big-endian `{tag, record count, trailer size}` then 60-byte records of floats/ints and a word trailer; file size is exactly `12 + 60 * count + trailer`). Verified on all 134 `v1b` files (27 are empty 12-byte stubs). Written as `<file>.json` with the words unnamed. The 6 other files in the folder are not `v1b`. |
| **BGDT stage background tables** | Wii / Stage data | ✅ | ❌ | *Teolsil Kirby Iyagi* (Korean *Kirby's Epic Yarn*) `stage/stage*/bg.dat` (`BGDT`, big-endian `{tag, 1, 0, capacity 16, record count}` then 52-byte records; file size is exactly `20 + 52 * count` in all 51 files: 72 bytes = 1 record, 852 bytes = 16 records). Written as `<file>.json` with the words unnamed. |
| **SSRT parameter database** | Wii / Game data | ✅ | ❌ | *Sonic & Sega All-Stars Racing* `Resource/*.dat` (`SSRT`, big-endian, self-describing): header block at the offset stored at +8 (`{table hash, name[0x40], item count, item offsets}`), items `{field count, item hash, name[0x40], count * {name hash, type hash, value}}` chained back to back, then a NUL separated string table and the end marker `0xdeadbeef`. Type hashes `a45c088e` (float), `fbe46712` (int) and `b7459825` (string, value = absolute file offset) are decoded; `1e2a64fb` values (hashed ids) are kept as numbers. Field names exist only as hashes. Verified on all 15 `SSRT` files (e.g. 100 missions / 7000 fields in `MissionParams.dat`, 88 items in `Shopping.dat`). Written as `<file>.json`. |
| **ATCD text catalog** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | *Safar'Wii* `2d/<lang>/text/text_*.dat` (`ATCD`, big-endian `{tag, 0, hint, entry stride, entry count, 0x20, ...}`; entries are a 0x20-byte ASCII key plus u32 words; words pointing into the string area behind the entry table are NUL-terminated UTF-16BE strings). All 30 `text_*.dat` files of the 5-language disc are recognised by `wszst extract` (97 staff entries, 875 event entries in `text_event.dat`); the decoded Spanish strings read as real text. Written as `<file>.json`; words that are not string offsets stay numbers. |
| **EqGUI image sets** | Wii / GUI atlas | ✅ | ❌ | *Mary King's Riding School 2* `data/gui/imagesets/*.imageset.bin`: raw C-struct dumps (0xcc/0xcd fill bytes), `{char name[0x40], char texture path[0x100], u16 width, u16 height, u8 flag, u8 pad, u16 image count}` then `{char name[0x40], u16 x, y, w, h}` per image; the file size is exactly `0x148 + 0x48 * count` in all 18 retail files. Written as `<file>.json` (e.g. `Perfect` at 1,14, 127x37 in the 1024x768 `Comments` sheet). Matched by the `.imageset.bin` suffix. |
| **WSC shot scenarios** | Wii / Game data | ✅ | ❌ | *WSC Real 09 - World Snooker Championship* `shots/brokenshots/data/*.dat`: little-endian, exactly 2070 bytes: `char name[0x20]` (`2downandcueball`, `tricks\\1 UP 2 DOWN.dat`, ...), three u16 header words and 127 records of four LE words (the first 16 are ball positions `{x, y, radius 1.125, 0}`). All 54 files in the folder are recognised. The remaining records and the header words are not understood; written as `<file>.json` with the words unnamed (floats where the value looks like one). |
| **Atlus PAK** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Trauma Center: Second Opinion* / *New Blood* (`title.pak`, `model/mdl_lesion.pak`, `operation/*.pak`, ...): sequence of members, each a 0x100-byte header `{char name[0xfc] (may contain "/"), u32 LE size}` + payload padded to 0x40, ended by a 0x100-byte terminator (the walk must cover the file exactly). Payloads are mostly LZ10 (`10` + 24-bit size) and are unpacked while extracting, so `.tpx` -> TPL -> PNG and `.brres` -> GLB chain on; duplicate names get `~<index>`. All 15 retail paks of New Blood walk exactly; `tests/mk_atluspak.py` (PASS) |
| **49Games PAK** | Wii / Archive | ⚠️ | ❌ | *Summer Athletics* `.pak` (`0xDEADBEEF`, u32 LE count, index offset, version 1; 40-byte records `{hash, +8 offset, +16 stored size, +24 unpacked size}` at 0x14). Stored members extract with magic-based extensions (THP/WSBL/U8 chain on); members whose stored size differs from the unpacked size use an unidentified codec and are written as `.packed`. `tests/mk_ws49pak.py` (PASS) |
| **49Games WSBL** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | *Summer Athletics* sound banks (`"WSBL"`, BE `{version 1, count, data size}`, 0x8c-byte records at 0x14: channels `+0x08`, rate `+0x0c`, data offset `+0x14`, samples `+0x18`, DSP coefficients `+0x34` (second set `+0x30` later); ADPCM data block at the end of the file; stereo = two halves). Each channel becomes a standard `.dsp` -> WAV; all 19 banks of `sg08.pak` extract; `tests/mk_wsbl.py` (PASS) |
| **PAKb** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Secret Files: Tunguska* / *Puritas Cordis* `data.pak` (`"PAKb"`, u32 BE count, 12-byte `{name hash, offset, size}` records, payloads stored back to back from `8 + count * 12`; the last member is a name table of `{u32 hash, u32 length, name}` records (4,731 names, sorted by hash, covering about 63% of the 7,532 members), so named members are written under their real name (`barkeeper.lua`, `bicycle.dlg_bin`, ..., `~<n>` before the extension for repeated hashes) and the rest as `<hash>_<index>.<ext>` with the extension from the payload magic; the probe runs ahead of the loose `.pak` sniffers, one of which used to swallow the file without writing anything). Streamed member by member because the file (586 MB) exceeds the in-memory limit; all 7,532 members of the US disc extract (THP movies and U8 archives continue recursively); `tests/mk_pakb.py` (PASS) |
| **Mobiclip (`.mo`, `.moflex`, `.mods`)** | DS / 3DS / Wii / Video | ✅ | ✅ | Decoded to an MP4 preview (`<name>.d/<name>.mp4`) by the external `mobipeg` tool (an ffmpeg build with the Mobiclip codec and muxers); `CREATE` re-encodes an edited preview back into the same container generation and copies the original audio. Needs `mobipeg` on PATH; THP is handled the same way |
| **Opoona SL01 / OPKG** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | ArtePiazza *Opoona* `.btl` / `.bin` / `.dat` / `.map` members: `SL01` + u32le unpacked size + u32le packed size + a plain LZO1X stream. The data is usually an OPKG package (no magic: u32le count, u16le 0x20, u16le 0x20, count × 0x20-byte records `{u32le offset, u32le size, char name[24]}`; empty "NULL" slots are skipped). `.mdl` / `.tex` / `.mol` and the `system/{coating,effect,force}.bin` tables are raw (unwrapped) packages: a character model package is `{.mdl, .tex (TPL bundle), .tmp}`, a `.mol` holds the `.mot` clips |
| **Opoona textures (.tpl)** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | Standard Wii TPLs whose magic word was blanked (0-3), optionally in a 0x40-byte `TPLE` bundle (count 3, stride at 0x14, name at 0x1c); decoded to PNG through the normal TPL path; an edited `foo.tpl.d/foo.tpl.png` is re-encoded and its texels written back into `foo.tpl` (same format and size) |
| **Opoona model (.mdl)** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | `DHDM` / `TMDM` / `BODM` / `SMDM` / `TSDM` chunks (reversed FourCCs, big-endian size). Nodes (`BODM`, 0xc0 bytes: translation, s32 euler where 65536 = 360°, scale, first-child / next-sibling links, name offset into `TSDM`), materials (`TMDM`, 0x110 bytes: diffuse at 0xc0, texture index u16 at 0x104), meshes (`SMDM` → parts with a bone palette and vertex groups: type 0x9b strip / 0x93 list for skinned vertices `{pos, normal, u16 uv/4096, weights}`, 0x9a / 0x92 for the static 36-byte map vertices). Exported to a textured, skinned GLB (`<name>.glb`) with every sibling `<stem>.mot` / `*.mol` clip as an animation. Layouts come from the game loader (`main.dol`) and were checked against 4,600+ retail models |
| **Opoona animation (.mot)** | Wii / Animation | ✅ | ❌ | Header 0x30 (frames, fps, node count, footer = size − 32) then one 0x50-byte record per node `{u16 n_trans, n_rot, n_scale; mask; rot_ptr; bind t / trans_ptr; bind q; bind s / scale_ptr}`; keys are 8 bytes: rotation s16 × 4 / 4096 (quaternion), translation and scale s16 × 3 × d / 32768 with d the fourth u16. The trailing table is a sorted node-name hash → index list. Clips become glTF animations (30 fps) |
| **Opoona map (.map)** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | `RDHF`-headed chunk stream: `LDMF` lists SL01-packed model packages, `XETF` the shared SL01 TPL textures, `MANF` the names. Textures are written as `tex_NN.tpl[.png]`, models as `<name>.glb`; the placement / collision chunks are not interpreted |
| **Opoona message bank (msg_*.bin)** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | `(id, offset)` table behind a count word; each string is two tag bytes (speaker / style) plus NUL-terminated UTF-8. Written as `strings.txt` (`id<TAB>tag<TAB>text`, control bytes as `\xNN`) |
| **Opoona collision mesh (.atb)** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | Big-endian: header `{size, 0x800000, 0x20200000, ntri, tri/vert/normal/grid/list offsets}`, inverse cell sizes + minimum corner, float vertices and normals, `{v0, v1, v2, normal}` u16 triangles, a 32 x 32 u16 grid of offsets into 0xFFFF-terminated triangle lists. Written as `<name>.atb.obj`; the wall trailer after the lists (`u32 {size, count, 12}` + 24-byte `{f32 x1, z1, x2, z2, s16 y_top, s16 y_bottom, u16 angle, u16 flags}` records) is written as `<name>.atb.walls.obj`: one vertical quad per wall between (x1, z1) and (x2, z2) spanning the two heights, with the facing angle (65536 = 360 degrees) and flags as comments (the actor collision code only tests a wall when the mover is between the heights and heading within 0x5000 of the angle, ignores walls with `flags & 0xE000 == 0x8000` and takes the wall type as `flags & 0xDFFF`); the 32 x 32 grid is a spatial index: cell `i` is column `i % 32`, row `i / 32` from the minimum corner with sides `1 / inverse cell size`, and holds the offset (in u16 units) of its 0xFFFF-terminated triangle list. On 786,382 cell/triangle pairs over 321 files the listed triangle's bounding box overlaps its cell in 99.66% of cases (empty cells hold offsets past the list area). It is not written out. Verified on all 375 `map/**/*.atb` (indices in range; `[system]/all.atb` is a different layout and skipped) |
| **Opoona parameter tables (`*_param.bin`, `encounter.bin`)** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Members of `system/param.bin`: `char name[6]`, u16be count, u32 0, then `count` fixed-size records (size = `(file size - 12) / count`; 33 of the 37 tables fit, `map_link`, `pc`, `word` and `work` do not and stay raw). `wszst xx` writes `records.tsv` with, per record, the NUL-terminated ASCII / Shift-JIS strings (`@offset=text`, non-ASCII bytes as `\xNN`) and the non-zero u16be words (`@offset=value`); the per-table field meaning is not decoded except for `item_param.bin`, which also gets `items.tsv` (id, after-use item, category, name, buy/sell price, flag bytes, tag, two values, description). `job`, `style`, `personality`, `shopp`, `bonbon_rank`, `condition`, `license`, `situation`, `code`, `cmn_hkey`, `catalog`, `force` and `enemy` tables also get `table.tsv` (id plus their text slots, multi-line descriptions joined; `shopp` and `bonbon_rank` add a u32 value; `enemy` lists its name, two item names and six attack names, the numeric stats stay in `records.tsv`) |
| **Spyro: Dawn of the Dragon `.KCS`** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | `STREAMS/REVn.KCS` animation buffers: big-endian chunks `{u32 size, u32 size, u32 type, u32 count}` separated by 0xcd / zero padding. | **RKV (RK33)** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Krome `.rkv` archives (*Happy Feet Two*, *Legend of Spyro*, *Owls of Ga'Hoole*): `RK33` header with a trailing 0x60-byte-entry table; members raw or LZF (method byte 2); embedded FSB sound banks recurse |
| **MPH SNDFILE archive** | DS / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Metroid Prime Hunters* `.arc` (`SNDFILE\0`, big-endian header): `u32 count`, `u32 total size`, 16 reserved bytes, then 64-byte records `{char name[32], u32 offset, u32 padded size, u32 size}`, payloads 32-byte aligned. Members extract by name and recurse. Layout from NoneGiven's MphRead |
| **MPH Model** | DS / Model | ✅ | ❌ | *Metroid Prime Hunters* `*_Model.bin` (no magic, recognised by validated header, node, material, mesh and display-list bounds, ahead of the magic-less SPICA containers): packed Nitro GX display lists with the header's `ScaleBase << ScaleFactor` model scale, per-mesh material, and 2/4/8-bit paletted, A5I3, A3I5 and direct-colour textures (from the model or the sibling `*_Tex.bin`) written as `<stem>_t<N>_p<M>.png` beside the `.glb`. | **Scribblenauts `index.bin` / `.p`** | Wii U / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Scribblenauts Unlimited* (big-endian): `index.bin` = `u32 count`, `count` x `{u8 pack, u8 zlib level, u32 offset, u32 stored size, u32 size}` (pack 0xff = no data), then `u32 npacks` + length-prefixed `.p` names. Each `.p` starts with its file size and 0x40 bytes of header; members are zlib streams (+1 trailer byte counted in the stored size) or raw. The table has no names, so members are written as `NNNNN.bin` (`Gfx2` members as `.gtx`) and recurse; all 28,923 populated entries of the retail disc extract (2,470 entries have no data). Written as `<name>.bin.svg`, one path per colour in z order. Layout from GMMan's scrib-vecviz |

| **RenderWare ADPCM stream (`.rws`)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | *The Legend of Spyro: Dawn of the Dragon* `SPEECH/*.RWS` and `MUSIC/*/STRA*.RWS` (209 files): little-endian `0x80d` container with a `0x80e` header and a `0x80f` data chunk (marker `0x1c020065`, the chunk layout of the *How to Train Your Dragon* `.RWS`). Mono speech files become `NNN.dsp` per segment (only the first has an exact length, the others use their frame count), stereo music `ch0.dsp` / `ch1.dsp` in 0x4000-byte blocks (the stored interleave 0x8000 is the cycle; left / right correlation 0.58, against about 0 for the other block sizes tried); decoded to WAV by the DSP extractor. Not verified against a reference decoder |

Ubisoft IMA streams (codec 3: a version header, 10 PCM frames, then one nibble pair per frame) are decoded to `<id>.wav`, covering *Avatar* (`0x220015`). *Just Dance* music streams (codec 1: 16 bit little-endian PCM, channels interleaved, in the external `.spk`) are written as `<id>.wav`; all 76 PCM and 42 DSP sounds of the retail disc extract. `tests/audit_ubibao.py` compares every output with vgmstream-cli sample for sample: all 1,997 sounds of the *Avatar* disc (294 packages) and all 118 of *Just Dance* are bit-identical |

| **Next Level .zlib / NLOC** | Wii / Compression, Text | ✅ | ✅ | *Mario Strikers Charged*: `.zlib` = u32 BE size + zlib stream (`*.sanim.zlib` skeletal animations), written as `<file>.d/<name>`; `NLOC` `.loc` string tables (`{id, offset}` table + UTF-16BE pool) exported as `strings.txt`; `wszst compress <file> -d <name>.sanim.zlib` writes the BE size + zlib wrapper (`NLOC` string tables are still decode-only) |

A texture is a 0x20-byte big-endian header `{mip levels, kind, 4 colour-depth bytes, width @0xe, height @0x10}` followed by GX-tiled levels, largest first: kind 1 RGB5A3, kind 2 CMPR, kind 3 RGBA8. `wszst xx` writes the base level of each as `<file>.<hash>.png` (2,037 textures from 356 of the 390 files on the disc, character portraits and UI art checked by eye). The remaining 34 files are empty stubs or fonts with a different header; the non-texture entries of `.res` / `.dmn` and the rest of the Next Level formats (`.shier`, `.cph`, `.cam`, `.trg`, `.fen`, `.nis`, `.resbun`) are not decoded |

Each sound is written as `<NN>.dsp` and converted to WAV by `wszst xx`: 944 sounds from 48 of the 54 banks, bit-exact against an independent reference decoder on one bank. Stream banks (`FE_GEN_Music`, `STREAM_GEN_Music`, `STREAM_GEN_Crowd`, `STREAM_GEN_NIS`: no wave table, chunk `0x23202` lists `{index, offset, size, hash, channels, 0, 0}` entries) hold one complete Next Level `IDSP` per entry, written as `<NN>.idsp` and then split into `chN.dsp` / WAV by the IDSP extractor (bit-exact against vgmstream on three tracks of `FE_GEN_Music`); the empty `HUD_*_Music` banks have no data. Loop points of the sound-effect banks are not exposed |

All 17 Pikmin sequences render (note ranges and bar counts sane; table loads use the 32 bit register pairs; child tracks are per parent). `demobgm` is a port dispatcher: one short file per selector (1, 2, 6, 8, 9, 10, 12). | **Trauma Team `.tpx`, `.bf`, `.bm2`** | Wii / texture pack, script | ✅ | ❌ | `.tpx` records (flags, offset, size, name) point at standard TPLs, written out and decoded to PNG (all 310 sampled files, 387 textures); `.bm2` bare `MSG2` message files (all 38 sampled) and `.bf` Atlus `FLW0` flow scripts → text with the proc/jump labels and every Shift-JIS message string of the embedded `MSG2` block (instruction stream and message control codes not interpreted) |

| **Nippon Ichi IMY (`.imy`)** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | *Phantom Brave: We Meet Again* `IMY\0` textures (little-endian 0x20-byte header: decoded size, row bytes, flags, 4 / 8 / 32 bits per pixel, height, palette entries; RGB565 or RGB5A3 palette words, then a control-byte stream of literal runs, literal back-references and left / above / above-left / above-right copies over 2- or 4-byte units; the result is GX C4 / C8 / RGBA8 tile order padded to whole tiles). `wszst xx` writes `<name>.imy.png`. All 972 members of the three archives convert; the DSP decoder is the hardware-exact one used elsewhere. The stereo `.wsd` form that vgmstream describes does not occur on this disc. The non-image parts of the other `.dat` files on the disc (`anm*`, `mp*`) are game-specific tables and are not decoded |
| **Phantom Brave battle map (`.mpd`)** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | *Phantom Brave: We Meet Again* `mp*.mpd` (big-endian, layout from the game's own map loader): 0x40-byte header (u16 count A @0, u16 count B @4, u32 byte size of a trailing u16 table @0x30), a 64x64 grid of 4-byte cells stored column by column, A records of 0x40 bytes, B records of 0x24 bytes, the u16 table, then up to 4 KiB of further 16-byte records. `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` with the header, the four grid byte planes and every record as hex. | **Infernal ITL** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `.itl` stereo DSP music (*MX vs. ATV Untamed*): each channel's blocks start with its own 0x60 header, 0x10000 interleave, final blocks only as long as the remaining data; split into `chN.dsp`/WAV, verified bit-exact against vgmstream on 22 files. | **tri-Crescendo CAF** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `CAF ` block-chained stereo music (*Fragile Dreams*, extensionless files / `.caf` / `.cfn`): every 0x80000-byte block carries its own channel chunks and DSP coefficients; the chunks are concatenated into 32 kHz `ch0.dsp`/`ch1.dsp` with the loop block turned into loop points; verified bit-exact against vgmstream on 9 files, all 47 retail files parse |

| **Capcom MT Framework Wii serialized object (`.xfs`)** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.xfs` (`\0SFX`, version 8): class table at `0x10 + u32@0x10`, each class a `{crc, info}` pair with `info >> 17` 0x38-byte members `{name offset (from 0x10), type, flags, size, defaults}`; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing every class and member (type / flags / size), e.g. `mQuality`, `mpAttributeSeData`. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii attack table (`.atk`)** | Wii / Combat Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.atk` (`\0KTA`, version 0x1021): 8-byte header then `count` 176-byte attack records `{name[8], flags[4], attr[4], damage f32 @0x10, ...}`; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing each attack name, damage, flags, and attributes. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii weapon parameters (`.wpd`)** | Wii / Combat Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.wpd` (`\0DPW`, 20 bytes): a u32 (0, 0x205 or 0x20a) and six big-endian u16 values; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt`. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii sound tables (`.qrts` / `.qers`)** | Wii / Audio Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 stream tables (`QRTS`, version 0x1a; 10 files: `bgm.qrts` names its 202 BGM streams, matching the count word @8) and sound-effect tables (`QERS`, version 0x13; 1,308 files, each naming its `.dspw` bank): fixed-size records (not decoded) followed by NUL-terminated `sound\...` path strings. `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` with the header words and every path in order (the first 300 `.qers` all yield their path) |
| **Capcom MT Framework Wii effect sprite animation (`.ean`)** | Wii / Animation | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.ean` (`\0NAE`, date `0x20060501`): u32 body size @8 (= file size - 16), u32 animation count @0xc, 0x20-byte records at 0x10 `{u32 rect list offset (from 0x10), u16 frame count, u16 kind, 6 words}` and then the 8-byte `{x, y, w, h}` rect lists back to back to the end of the file (all 27 retail files tile exactly; cells such as 96 x 96, 5 per row). `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt`; the meaning of the record words is inferred, not confirmed |
| **Capcom MT Framework Wii actor index (`.idx`)** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.idx` (`\0XDI`): u32 count @4, followed by `count` u32 mapping IDs and `count` 20-byte record descriptors (u32[5]); `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing each entry. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii scripted resource (`.scra`)** | Wii / Scripting | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.scra` (`SCRA`): u16 version @4 (7), u16 count @6, followed by `count` 8-byte resource descriptors `{u32 type_hash, u32 resource_hash}`; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing referenced resource hashes. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii surround sound (`.srnd`)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.srnd` (`SRND`): u32 version @4 (2), u32 count @8, followed by `count` 140-byte sound records `{u32 sound_id, u32 flags, ...}`; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing surround sound parameter definitions. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii sound event request (`.qers`)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.qers` (`QERS`): u32 version @4 (19), u32 count @8, followed by `count` 144-byte sound event records and a trailing string table containing sound bank paths; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing sound events and bank paths. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii collision table (`.col`)** | Wii / Physics & Collision | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.col` (`\0LOC`, version 2): u16 version @4, u16 count @6, followed by `count` collision group headers and 64-byte shape chunks; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing collision groups and shape sub-chunks. | **Capcom MT Framework Wii AI decision trees (`.thk`)** | Wii / AI & Logic | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.thk` (`\0KHT`): u32 version @4, u32 count @8, followed by `count` tree headers and 176-byte behavior node records; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.txt` listing decision trees and node counts.
