| **Capcom MT Framework Wii animation SRT table (`.astl`)** | Wii / Animation | ✅ | ❌ | Sengoku Basara 3 `.astl` (`ASTL`): wraps NW4R BRRES (holding `AnmTexSrt` / `SRT0`) at offset `0x144` (size `0x140`); unwrapped to `<name>.brres` |
| **Havok-style HVT texture** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | *Aladdin Magic Racer* `texWii/*.hvt`: 0x18-byte big-endian header `{" IVH", version 2 or 6, format tag, width, height, bits per pixel}` then a plain Wii GX pixel chain without mips: `S3TW` = CMPR, `ARGB` = RGBA8, `G8A8` = IA8, `GRY8` = I8; `wszst xx` writes `<name>.hvt.png` (all 507 textures of the retail `data.hvp` decode; one of each variant checked by eye). The `.hvp` packs themselves are handled by the HVP extractor; Octomania `.wt`/`.pcm` banks are not decoded |
| **Retro PAK / CSMP** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Retro Studios PAK v2 (*Metroid Prime 3*, *Donkey Kong Country Returns* with zlib blocks): STRG names + RSHD table + DATA, `CMPD` blocks of LZO1X pieces unpacked (all 14 retail paks decode), `TXTR` decoded to PNG in decode mode (I4–RGBA8, CI4/CI8, CMPR); `.csmp` samples become `.dsp`/WAV (150 of 203 checked bit-exact against vgmstream, the rest differ only by its looping); `.strm` is FSB3; `.ras` (`RAS_`, DKCR music, interleaved DSP with block+sample loop points) split into `chN.dsp`/WAV, exact up to the loop end against vgmstream; `.strg` string tables (v3) become one `<LANG>.txt` each |
| **Petz TTPL** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | Petz-engine texture (*Petz Sports*, *Petz Dogz/Horsez/Catz* family; extension-less files under `3d/texture/`): an 8-byte `{"TTPL", size}` wrapper around a standard Wii TPL, decoded to `<name>.png` by `wszst xx`. Level containers `.ozzy` (`u32 0`, zlib chunks `{u32 raw, u32 comp, data}`, 4 byte trailer) are inflated to `level.bin`; the embedded `TTPL` textures (named by the preceding `TEXT` record) and every `GEOM` (version 3/4: attribute blocks `{count, 0, stride, desc, size}` for f32 / s16 / s8 positions, s8 normals, u8 colours and u8 / s16 texcoords with fixed point fraction bits, then the GX triangle display list and the diffuse texture path) are written as `<name>.ttpl` / PNG and `models.glb` (all 42 *Petz Sports* levels; triangle winding verified against the stored normals). Standalone `3d/mesh/**` `MESH` records (geometry path, texture path) and bare `GEOM` files become `model.glb` plus the referenced `...txl` TTPL. Skinned meshes `SMSH` / `FSMH` (19-byte attribute records, then one GX display list per bone batch of `{u8 matrix index, f32 position, [f32 normal], f32 texcoord}`) are exported as a skinned `.glb` (47 / 67 joints from the node table in its second, bind pose, weight palettes as joint weights; the trailing block `{u32 count, count x {u32 n, n x u16 palette index}}` has one list per display list, and a vertex's GX matrix index / 3 picks the palette; it ends exactly at the end of the file on three dogs; the mirrored left / right legs of that pose are symmetric, the first pose is not); `wszst xx` also writes `<name>.skeleton.txt` with the node table (88-byte records `{id hash, scale, translation, rotation xyzw, a second scale / translation / rotation, child count}` in depth first order, so the child counts give the parent links; they sum to N - 1 and the hash set equals the one of the sibling `_mact`, checked on a 47 and a 67 node dog, bone names are not stored), followed by the skin weight palettes `{u32 count}` then `{u32 n, n x id hash, n x f32 weight}` (weights sum to 1 and every hash is a node, checked on three dogs: 200, 481 and 389 palettes), listed in the same file `MACT` animations (bit-packed per-bone tracks) and `DPGZ` displacement tracks are not decoded |
| **Petz TEXL** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | Petz-engine texture (*Cranium Kabookii*; extension-less files under `3d/texture/`): `TEXL` header then raw GX pixels, decoded to `<name>.png` by `wszst xx`. CMPR, RGBA8 and CMPR with a separate I4 alpha plane (`_atxl`) variants; other variants are left untouched |
| **Heavy Iron HO** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Good Engine `.ho` packages (*WALL-E*, *Up*, also *Ratatouille* on other platforms): big-endian MAST / SECT / PSL / PSLD tables; assets written as `name.<type hash>` with debug names from PSLD; GX texture blobs (RGB5A3 / RGBA8 / CMPR) → PNG. The `.lo` text log beside each package lists asset type names |
| **Asobo BigFile** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Asobo Studio "Internal Cross Technology" `.DRV` volumes (*Ratatouille*): big-endian v1.06.63 blocks, stored or LZRS members, hashed names (Asobo CRC) with class names (`Bitmap_Z`, `Mesh_Z`, ...); `Bitmap_Z` (RGB565 / RGBA8 / CMPR) → PNG; `Sound_Z` (Nintendo DSP-ADPCM with a standard DSP header) → WAV; `Mesh_Z` (Wii GX display lists over s16 positions / s16 UVs / s8 normals) → GLB with one primitive per material and the material's first bitmap as texture (static bind pose; `Skin_Z` skinning and `Node_Z` transforms are not applied). Other classes stay raw. Format after widberg/bff |
| **Bj engine** | Wii / Assets | ✅ | ❌ | *Super Karts* (Pro Kart): little-endian `.tx1` headers + `.tx2` 8-bit paletted pixels (2 KiB-unit chunks, square mip chains) to PNG; `.mtm` mesh trees (node hierarchy, 4x4 matrices, indexed list/strip/fan primitives, UVs) to textured GLB; `.bsi` big-endian sound banks (mono PCM8) and `.bsm` planar-stereo PCM8 music at 32 kHz to WAV. `.3df` 3D fonts (*Crash Car Racer* `Fonts/m1`; `BjFont3DActivate`: u32 0, u32 flags, 256 glyph mesh offsets) to one GLB with a mesh per character on a 16-column grid; a `.tx2` with a valid sibling `.tx1` is claimed (its pixel bytes can pass for an LZX header). Layout recovered from the disc's symbolised ELF. |
| **Torus Games hunkfile** | Wii / Assets | ✅ | ❌ | *Barbie & Her Sisters: Puppy Rescue* `.hnk`: little-endian chunks `{u32 size, u16 kind, u16 0}` (low 12 bits of kind = record type, upper bits = memory pool) ending exactly at EOF; `0x71` names each asset (class + name), `0x150`/`0x151` = `TSETexture` header (big-endian width/height/mips) + GX mip chain (CMPR / RGBA8 / I8, told apart by chain size) to PNG; `0x92`/`0x93` = `SqueakStream` (`IWAR` header, big-endian samples/rate, DSP coefficients at +64 / +112) naming a raw DSP-ADPCM file in `../SOUND` to WAV (stereo assumed split in file halves). `RenderModelTemplate`, `Animation`, `Clank*`, `.files` manifests and `.mo` movies are not decoded |
| **Atomic Planet PUB** | Wii / Assets | ✅ | ❌ | *AMF Bowling: Pinbusters!* `*WII.PUB`: big-endian nested section headers, objects from 0x40 plus a trailing table of 28-byte `{hash, offset, class, size}` entries; class 1 textures carry a palette offset/count, mip count, GX format and `{offset, size, height, width}` per mip level (height first); C8 (IA8 / RGB565 / RGB5A3 palettes), RGBA8 and I8 to PNG. Class 8 meshes: a root at +0x74 lists per-part display lists (GX strips / lists / quads with u16 indices per attribute), texture name hashes and attribute slot records (position f32x3, colour RGBA8, UV s16x2 / 1024, normal s16x3 / 16384); exported as GLB with the diffuse PNG by hash. `effects`, `strap` and `SFX` packages use a different header and are left as-is. |
| **Humongous Resource.rez** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Backyard Football '10* `Resource.rez`: big-endian 24-byte slots `{offset, size, unpacked size, type, flags}`; a footer sector gives the slot count and the slot table sits before it. Group slots (flags = table size) end in a resource table; resources are byte-LZ compressed (flag bit 0; u32 size, then `0x80|n` literal runs and `n, u16 distance` back-references, from `Decompress` in `Release.elf`). Type 73 textures (width, height, GX format 5/6/8/9, RGB5A3 palettes) to PNG; type 76 sounds (`{6, rate, 0, data bytes}`, 16 coefficients at +0x44, DSP-ADPCM frames from +0x80) to WAV; slots that are not groups (548 more textures, 26 sounds, 6 `THP` videos) are handled too. Group ids are slot index + the footer's first slot (50). Type 75 models are the game's depth-first object tree (224-byte header with rest transform and pivot, GX display list, position / colour / normal / UV arrays, batches naming their texture by `group << 16 | index`, children after 32-byte padding); vertices are in world space. Type 86 is the matching animation tree (40-byte keyframes: position, quaternion, scale) exported as glTF translation / rotation / scale channels at 30 fps relative to each object's rest pose about its pivot. Skinned models (55 resources: player parts on a 29-bone skeleton) export with a skin in bind pose: per position 3 bone indices + weights, bone tree of local offsets. Type 7 motions (61 frames x 29 bones: root offset f32x3 + s16 quaternions / 32767 per frame, stride 0xf4) become skeleton-only GLBs using the joints of the first skinned model, root offset relative to frame 0, 30 fps. Types 24, 44, 46, 52 are not decoded. `release.rcf` / `release.dsf` on the disc are signed network-update blobs, not game data. |
| **BombShell data pack** | Wii, PC / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Bee Movie Game* `.xwi` (Wii, big-endian, 32-byte aligned) and `.xdx9` (PC, little-endian): one memory image `0x020100a0, 0x040100af, 0x138, n` with 24-byte directories `{-, type (1 interface, 3 link, 4 world), -, -, size, datapack offset}`; each datapack has a 192 (Wii) / 188 (PC) byte header (+4 sound bytes, +8 sound table words, +20 textures, +32 sounds, +108 word list). Sounds are `{data offset, size, rate, flags}` records whose data is a whole FSB3 bank (Wii) or RIFF WAVE (PC) with its source path right behind it. Textures are 32-byte records in an offset list (format byte 0x45 / 0xc5 / 0xc6 = CMPR on Wii where 0xc5 / 0xc6 keep alpha as a second CMPR image; 0x45 DXT1, 0xca DXT5, 0xa0 BGRA8, 0x18 BGR8 on PC; log2 width / height; pixel, aux, name and alpha offsets). Base level only. Layout from the Pac-Kart project. Wii models are found through the datapack's model patch list (`+56` entries `{u32 pointer, u16, u16}` at `datapack + u32(+0) + m`, where `m` is the patch list offset minus `u32(+0)`; all pointers add `m`): a model record `{u16, u16, u32 name pointer (Content\\shapes\\...), +16 list count, +20 list of {count, pointer array}}` whose sub-mesh records (224 bytes; type 0 static, 1 skinned-part, 2 and 3 not decoded) point at raw buffers: `+0xb0` vertex flags (bit 0 position, 1 normal, 2 colour, 3 UV: one big-endian u16 index each per vertex, in that order), `+180` float positions, `+188` (type 0) / `+184` (type 1) colours, `+192` float normals, `+196` s16 8.8 UVs and `+200` `{0, byte count, pointer}` to a GX display list `{command (0x90 triangles, 0x98 strip, 0xa0 fan, 0x80 quads), BE u16 count, vertices}`. A sub-mesh's texture is the patch list entry (`datapack + u32(+0) ...`: texture entries `{u32 pointer, u16, u16}` start at `m - 8 * (u32(+16) + u32(+52) + u32(+28) + u32(+84))`) whose pointer equals the field at sub-mesh `+48` (or `+56`); its second u16 is the texture index within the directory. Exported as one GLB per model in `DIR/models` (LOD 0, positions, normals, UV0, the PNG embedded per sub-mesh; no skeleton; degenerate strip joins dropped, some stray triangles remain). Not decoded: skinned weights and skeletons, types 2 / 3, PC models and world data. |
| **Blue Tongue TRB** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *de Blob 2* `.trb` (`TRB\0`, version 0x7d1, big-endian; not the TSFB layout of Barnyard): 0x30-byte section descriptors at 0x80 `{0, name offset, 0, flags, size, size, file offset}` (section 0 is the string table; `.data`, `__s00000` pixel pool, `gpu_data`, `model_collision`, `XUR`), then 16-byte symbols `{tag, offset, section << 16, name offset}`. A `ttex` symbol is a texture object: `0x0df00bb0` fill, then GX format (0 I4, 1 I8, 2 IA4, 3 IA8, 5 RGB5A3, 6 RGBA8, 8 C4, 9 C8, 14 CMPR), pixel offset in the pool at +0xc, palette offset +0x14 and format +0x18, stored size +0x1c, width / height pairs at +0x40 (+0x44 in UI files; first pair is the stored size) and the `.tga` name; only the base level is decoded. The `XUR` section is written as a `.xur` file. `tcmd` symbols are models: +0x10 batch count, +0x14 offset of a batch offset array; a 0x68-byte batch record holds the display list (size +0x40, offset +0x44 in `gpu_data`) and `{array offset, attribute << 24 \| element size << 16 \| count}` pairs for positions (s16x3, fraction bits from the batch's format table), normals (s8x3), TEX0 and TEX1 (s16x2); GX triangle / strip / fan / quad primitives with one u8 (u16 above 256 entries) index per attribute. Exported as one GLB per model (positions, normals, UVs, diffuse material). The format table `{n, n x {b0, b1, fraction bits, attribute}}` (from the record's +4 offset) lists every vertex attribute; entries below 9 are inline index bytes with no array, and the `{array offset, descriptor}` pairs (record +0x14; display list size and offset are the two words before them) exist only for the attributes a batch owns or shares (colour arrays are indexed but not exported). A count with bit 15 set means the array is not stored with the batch: the extractor loads every model package next to the `.trb` (first `LevelAssets.trb`) and takes the arrays of the same-named model, trying that model's batches with the same index first and then the others until every index of the display list fits (an instance in a cell may order its batches differently); a batch without a descriptor for an attribute continues with the array of the closest earlier batch of the model (terrain pieces share their position pool this way). About 98% of the batches of the *Ink Point* level cells export. Materials: the `CoreMaterialSets_Mem` entry named like the model lists `CoreMaterials_Mem` records (28 bytes, name first); word 2 (low 12 bits) indexes `GXSET_TexObj_Mem` (52-byte entries `0x06nnkk00`, then `nn` `{0, texture << 16}` pairs) and the texture number indexes the `ttlt` name table; entry 0 is the cell lightmap and the diffuse map is the first name that is not a spec / envmap / bump helper. TEX0 holds the lightmap coordinates (12 fraction bits), TEX1 the diffuse ones (8 fraction bits, v from -1 to 0, wrapping); the diffuse PNGs are written next to the GLBs. Not decoded: terrain cell models (`cellN_gM`, no material set), collision data, the lightmap as a second UV layer and the `CETB` compressed level. |
| **Toshi TSFB (TRB/TTL)** | Wii / Assets | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* (layouts after the OpenBarnyard decompilation): `TSFB` big-endian hunks `XRDH` header, `TCES`/`CCES` section (`CCES` = BTEC `CETB` LZ77 with 6/14-bit lengths and 7/15-bit offsets, optional XOR), `CLER` relocations, `BMYS` symbols `{u16 section, u16 name offset, u32 hash, u32 data offset}`. `.ttl` (symbol `TTL`): 13-word texture entries (format `0x300 +` I4..CI8_IA8, name, size, data/palette pointers, mip count) decoded to PNG (first level). `.tkl` (symbol `keylib`, `TKeyframeLibrary::TRBHeader`): quantized bone-animation keyframe pools (packed-s16 translations scaled per-axis, packed-s16 unit quaternions, float scales) decoded to YAML; verified against the OpenBarnyard decompilation and cross-checked on ~250 disc samples, except scale keyframes (no disc sample has any). `.trb` models (`Skeleton`/`Materials`/`Header`/`LOD0_Mesh_n` symbols) convert to skinned GLB with wmdlt: bones (local quaternion + position, world/inverse bind matrices), `LOD0` meshes as GX display lists of separately indexed s16 positions (fixed-point, frac bits in the mesh header), s8 normals and s16 texcoords, skin-blend table (up to 3 bones per entry) selected through the per-submesh matrix-slot table, material textures resolved from `Data/Matlibs/<model>.ttl` (verified on the disc: 892 of 895 model files convert, samples checked in Blender); the direct-UV variant (3 tiny UI props), LOD1+, collision and animation sequences are not decoded. `.trz` and `TSFL` little-endian files are not decoded |
| **Toshi TRZ** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* `.trz` property trees and the property-tree `.trb` files (interaction, activity-set and unit-type tables; the 1,074 `Main`-symbol `.trb` model-type definitions with their animation sets): a TSFB container whose `Main` symbol is an OpenBarnyard `PBProperties` tree (pointers are section offsets; name and value records, int/uint/float/bool/string/array/nested values), written as an indented YAML-style listing `<file>.yaml`. The three `.trz` files decode (2073, 2052 and 3183 properties) and so do 1,074 of the 1,085 `Main`-symbol `.trb` files with readable values such as function calls, animation names and spawn goals. |
| **Toshi TSO** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* `.tso` scripted sequences (cut-scenes; layout after OpenBarnyard `ASSeq`): a 24-byte header of string and pointer relocation tables, then a script with one instruction track per actor (actor events, animations, waits, voice-overs, fades, flags, pause/resume, named commands). Written as a YAML-style `<file>.yaml` listing each track's instructions with strings resolved through the relocation table; all 38 disc files decode (for example `bindCamera`, `SetTerrain`, animation names, flag sync). The raw words of actor-event and animation instructions are listed unlabelled because the decompilation does not name their fields. |
| **Toshi terrain** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* `Data/Terrain/<area>/*Mod<n>.trb` (symbols `Database`/`Header`/`Materials`/`Collision` only; LOD shader 2 = world, 4 = grass): thousands of static GX meshes, each a leaf of a bounding-sphere tree `{sphere (= origin of the s16 vertices), links, sphere, ptr positions, ptr texcoords, ptr colours, ptr ?, ptr display list, DL size, ptr material, vertex count, u8 fmt[4]}`; the position array pointer sits in the word before the first header word and the table in the file lists node ends (the first mesh is before it), so the meshes are found by scanning for headers whose material is a `Materials` entry. `fmt` = frac bits + per-attribute index width (1 = inline in the DL as s16 x3 + 2-byte colour + s16 x2, 2 = u8, 3 = u16); colours are RGB565, RGBA4444 when bit 7 of the colour width is set (grass). Vertex order is position, colour, texcoord. `wmdlt ENCODE` writes one GLB per section (Z-up to Y-up, vertex colours) plus the textures from the `<name>Mat.ttl` beside it; 893 of 895 disc files convert (the other two are empty). Inline-vertex meshes also fix the three `Data/Models` files (`in_banners`, `in_poster_1`, `wackring`) the model decoder used to skip. |
| **Toshi terrain locators** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* `Data/Terrain/<area>/*skel.trb` (layout after OpenBarnyard `ATerrainLocator.h`): `Locators` = `{s32 count, ptr locators, u32 name count, ptr name table}`, a locator is 32 bytes `{float quaternion xyzw, float position xyz, u16 name id, u8 flags1 (low nibble * 0.2 = scale, high nibble flags), s8 flags2}`; `LocatorVIS` = `{s32 count, ptr sections}` with sections `{ptr name, u32 first locator, u32 count}`. Written as `<file>.yaml` (all 64 disc skeleton files decode; 717 locators in `EnvBeadyFarm_skel.trb`). |
| **CLMP model** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | War Drum *Manhunt 2* `global/*.dff` (magic `CLMP`; relocatable image of bone names, `.gpl` geometry and `.tpl` texture pools). Each geometry pool starts with a mesh block `{u32 0x18, colour desc, texcoord desc array, normal desc, command list, u8 texcoord sets}`; array descriptors are `{u32 offset, u16 count, u8 type<<4 \| fraction bits, u8 components}`: positions and normals share one s16 array (6 components, fraction 14, normals at +6), texcoords are s16 pairs with fraction 11. The command list is `{?, ptr entries, u16 count}` of 16-byte entries `{u8 type, value, display list offset, size}`: type 1 = material (`value & 0x1fff`), type 3 = vertex format (2-bit index width per attribute from bit 2: position, normal, colour 0/1, texcoord 0..), every non-zero offset is a GX display list (quads, triangles, strip, fan) drawn in that state. Vertices are already in model space (T-pose figure, no skinning needed). `wmdlt ENCODE` writes an untextured GLB of every mesh block (all 14 `global` files and the unpacked level packs convert); this is the glTF route next to the `.obj` + skin/bone extraction of the *Manhunt 2 world and model geometry* row, and the textures come from the `.txd` beside the model. |
| **Toshi locale strings** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | Blue Tongue *Nickelodeon Barnyard* `Data/Locale/<lang>.trb` string tables (`LocaleStrings`: count plus pointers to UTF-16 strings, after OpenBarnyard `T2Locale`) written as a UTF-8 `<file>.txt` of `index<TAB>text` lines. All 8 languages decode (3,477 strings each, including the Japanese table). The `Data/Subs` subtitle libraries (`SubtitleVersion`) are not decoded: their line strings sit behind an extra pointer level that is not understood. | Subtitle libraries (`Data/Subs/<lang>/lib_*.trb`, symbol `SubtitleVersion`): two sections share one `TCES` hunk (XRDH lists their sizes: `{version, count, count x {?, size, ?, ?}}`), one symbol per sound in section 1 = `{s32 line count, count x {s32 start ms, s32 end ms, u32 ptr}}` with section-relative pointers to UTF-16BE strings; written as `[<sound>]` blocks of `start-end<TAB>text` in `<file>.txt` (the Japanese release's strings are all empty).
| **h.a.n.d. FBC bundle** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Miffy no Omochabako* `.fbc`: big-endian `00 14 00 06`, `u32 n`, six table offsets, then `n` member offsets at `0x40` (relative to `0x40`); the last `2n` 32-byte slots of the file hold the member sizes (`u32`, `0`-padded) then NUL-terminated names. Members back to back; nested bundles share the layout. Leaves are `.brres`, `.tpl`, `.brasd`, `.ssb` scripts and `csvbin`/`txtbin` message tables (last two not decoded) |
| **Vblank BFP / BAP** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | `gamedata_wii.bfp` (`BFP2`, little-endian): 16-byte `{hash, offset, size, stored}` members plus 256 numbered `{offset, size, stored}` slots; stored raw when `size == stored`, else zlib. Names are `h = (h << 1) ^ T[(h & 255) ^ upper(c)]` hashes (table in `lib-vblank.c`); the ~145 recovered names come from the game DOLs and package contents, others keep `<hash>.<ext>` with content-sniffed extensions. `audio_*_Wii.bap`: `BPP3` (*Shakedown*) sounds hold 1..n streams (kind `0x100` Ogg Vorbis → `.ogg`, kind `0x20` mono DSP-ADPCM with a big-endian DSP header → `.wav`) named after their title metadata; `BAP1` (*Retro City Rampage*) holds zlib-compressed `BTRK` tracker modules plus a raw 8-bit sample pool (`samples.raw`); the tracker/sample binding and the `.rec`/`.map`/`.til` game-data formats are not decoded. Layouts reversed from `main.dol` |
| **FMOD FSB** | Wii / Audio bank | ✅ | ❌ | FSB3 (*Nickelodeon Barnyard*; 0x18-byte header, same sample headers, 4-channel music) and FSB4 (*Up*, *WALL-E*, *Cars 2*, *Birthday Party Bash*; incl. basic-header banks) and FSB5 (*Brave*): DSP-ADPCM (mono / stereo, 2-byte interleave) and PCM16 samples → WAV; other codecs copied out as `.bin`. FSB3 not handled |
| **kRAW music** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | *Geometry Wars: Galaxies*: `"kRAW"`, u32 size, then big-endian s16 mono samples from +8 (separate `_Left` / `_Right` files per stereo pair; 32 kHz inferred from the 64.08 s loop lengths, not stored in the file). Written as `audio.wav`. `Level/*.bin` is not decoded (the `GameAudioDataWii.spd/.spt` bank is covered by the SPD/SPT row) |
| **Avalanche THB/TBB** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ✅ | Avalanche Software engine textures (*Cars 2*, inside the game's `.zip` / `.tszip` archives): big-endian `.thb` header (one or many textures: offset table + 32-byte records) + tiled GX `.tbb` pixels (I8 / RGB5A3 / RGBA8 / CMPR) → PNG; atlases write `name_0.png`, `name_1.png`, ...; an edited PNG is re-encoded into the `.tbb` (the `.thb` is only read) |
| **Battle of the Bands BAG** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Battle of the Bands* (Backbone Entertainment, Wii) `.bag` asset container. No public documentation found (checked Xentax/ZenHax archive mirrors, GitHub, Wii modding wikis). Reverse-engineered against all 215 `.bag` samples on the retail disc: fixed 32-byte ASCII header `"1.00 <N>\n"` (zero-padded; `N`'s exact meaning wasn't pinned down — it only equals the payload length for 5/215 samples) followed by a payload of concatenated sub-resources. Confirmed present in 215/215 samples: a plain-text manifest of `name,size,offset\n` CSV records (absolute file offsets; the first entry always starts at 32, right after the header) bracketed by runs of `*`, naming the embedded sub-resources — many of which (e.g. `buildall.bag`, `hudsceneobjects.bag`) are themselves raw zlib streams that were confirmed to inflate at their declared offsets. `lib-botbbag.c`/`.h` decode the header and every manifest record, and verify+size each zlib blob found at a record's offset; individual sub-resource payloads (GX/GFX texture data, level-geometry blobs of raw big-endian floats) were not further decoded, and this is detection-only — no `wszst EXTRACT` wiring yet. |
| **Havok HKX (classic packfile)** | Wii / Physics-Animation | ✅ | ❌ | *Tenchu: Shadow Assassins* (Acquire, Wii, 2009) ships Havok 4.6.1 skeleton/animation/ragdoll data under `Common/Motion/{Skeleton,Face}/*.HKX`. This is licensed Havok middleware, not a proprietary format, but the pre-2011 "classic packfile" binary layout (magic `0x57E0E057 0x10C0C010`) is undocumented by Havok itself, and every open-source Havok reimplementation checked ([HavokLib](https://github.com/PredatorCZ/HavokLib), [hkxparse](https://github.com/exyorha/hkxparse), hkxcmd) targets Havok 5.0+ or the 2010+ tagfile variant — none cover Havok 4.6.1. Reverse-engineered directly against retail samples (verified byte-for-byte, e.g. `skeleton_main.HKX`, `Face/face_ayame.HKX`): a fixed `hkPackfileHeader` (`userTag`, `fileVersion`, `layoutRules[4]` — observed `{4,0,1,1}`, i.e. 32-bit pointers, big-endian, matching the Wii target — `numSections`, contents-section indices, and a 16-byte `contentsVersion` string, e.g. "Havok-4.6.1-r1") followed by `numSections` fixed 48-byte `hkPackfileSectionHeader` entries (20-byte NUL-padded tag name, `absoluteDataStart`, then five offsets relative to it — localFixups/globalFixups/virtualFixups/exports/imports/endOffset — all equal when a section has no fixups, as in every section observed). Confirmed exactly: each section's `absoluteDataStart`/`endOffset` bounds the very next section's header, chained correctly end to end in every real sample checked. This packfile format is self-describing — the `__types__` section carries full `hkClass`/`hkClassMember` reflection metadata for every type in the file — so `lib-hkx.c`/`.h` decode the header, the complete section table (name/offset/size, for however many sections exist), and every embedded Havok class name from `__classnames__` (extracted by scanning for NUL-terminated ASCII runs starting `hk`/`hcl`, since the exact per-entry classnames record format wasn't pinned down — the class list alone identifies a file's content, e.g. `face_ayame.HKX`'s classes include `hkSkeleton`, `hkWaveletSkeletalAnimation`, and `hkBoneAttachment`, confirming it as compressed facial skeletal animation). The `__data__` section's binary content is not deserialized. Little-endian packfiles (`layoutRules[1] != 0`) are detected but not walked. Wired into `GetByMagicFF()` (exact two-word magic, no extension ambiguity) — detection-only, no `wszst EXTRACT` wiring. |
| **T4-RES (Tenchu: Shadow Assassins)** | Wii / Tagged Resource | ✅ | ❌ | *Tenchu: Shadow Assassins* reuses the generic `.b` extension for at least four unrelated internal layouts; this entry covers only the one carrying a real, self-identifying "T4-<TypeName>" ASCII tag (`Common/Camera/CameraSetDB.b`, `Common/Camera/{ayame,rikimaru}_camset.b`, `Common/AI/{AIVoiceCommon,AIStatusData,CMN_AI_scr}.b` — the only 6 real `.b` files on the disc carrying it). No public documentation exists. Confirmed structure: a 12-byte NUL-padded tag field (e.g. "T4-CamSetDB", "T4-AIVoice", "T4-AIScript", "T4-AIStatus", "T4-CamSet"), then 4 bytes of packed BCD that decode as a plausible authoring-tool export date (`{0x20,0x08,0x03,0x14}` → 2008-03-14) — the same era as the Havok 4.6.1 middleware embedded elsewhere on this disc. The record table following the header is type-specific (e.g. `CamSetDB`'s records are 12 bytes and open with an ASCII bone/character name like "RIKIMARU"; `AIVoice`'s differ) and was not reverse-engineered per type. `lib-t4res.c`/`.h` decode only the shared tag+date header. The disc's other `.b` layouts are explicitly out of scope for this entry: `*/Movie/*.b` movie-subtitle-cue tables (magic-less, a `u32` count header equal to 2× the following 16-byte record count, each record's last `u32` always `0xFFFFFFFF`, checked across all 108 real samples) and `Common/Event/*EventData.b` plain-count tables (`u32` count immediately followed by that many 16-byte records) were identified but are not decoded by any module, since both collide by extension with this format and with each other closely enough that magic-less detection would be unsafe. |
| **HDVOICE (Tenchu: Shadow Assassins)** | Wii / Audio Manifest | ✅ | ❌ | *Tenchu: Shadow Assassins* per-character voice-line manifest, `{FR,SP,US}/files/Sound/<CHARACTER>.hd` (102 real samples: `AYAME.hd`, `RIKIMARU.hd`, `COMMON.hd`, `MENU.hd`, `TUTORIAL.hd`, etc.). No public documentation exists. Magic-less, but structurally unique on this disc: all 102 real `.hd` files match the layout below with **zero leftover bytes**, the strongest confirmation available without an in-game reference to check field semantics against. Layout: an 8-byte header (`u32 total_slots`, `u32 group` — 0-3 observed, constant per file, meaning unconfirmed, candidate voice bus/mixer group) followed by `total_slots` 8-byte slots (`u16 category`, `u16 variant`, `s32 index`) — one per possible voice-line ID in this character's fixed address space; `index` is either that line's sequential position (0..n_real-1, strictly increasing in slot order — confirmed identical to slot order in every sample, i.e. it is redundant with position rather than an independent lookup) in the parameter table below, or the sentinel `-1` (`0xFFFFFFFF`) marking an ID not recorded in this localization (real/total ratios vary per character and per language, e.g. `AYAME.hd`: 200/542 real; consistent with per-language voice-line coverage gaps). Only categories 0/1/3 appear on real (non-sentinel) slots across the checked samples; 2/4 appear only on sentinel-only slots. Immediately after the slot table: `n_real` 44-byte parameter records, one per real slot in slot order (`self_index` — redundant, equals position; `volume` 0-127 MIDI-style, 127 dominant; two always-zero `u32`s; `pitch` `f32`, always exactly `1.0` in every sample checked; `group2` — constant `77` in nearly every file, `117` in `SEKISHO.hd`, `127` in `TUTORIAL.hd`, meaning unconfirmed; `priority` — 5/10/50 observed; two varying fields (0-100 range) whose meaning wasn't pinned down, candidates: delay/pan; `flag9` — always `1`; `flag10` — 0 or 1). `lib-hdvoice.c`/`.h` decode the complete structure (header, every slot, every parameter record); several individual field *meanings* above are inferred from value ranges/constancy rather than proven. Wired into `wszst`'s detection (both `GetFileTypeByMagic()` and the separate `cmd_filetype`/`FT` command path, which had its own independent, magic-heavy dispatch that this format's real samples could coincidentally collide with, e.g. `MENU.hd`/`COMMON.hd` matching a loose DSP-ADPCM heuristic before the explicit `.hd` structural check was added there too) — detection-only, no `wszst EXTRACT` wiring. |
| **Blitz REV** | Wii / GameCube / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Blitz Games "Babel" `.rev` (Wii) and `.gcp` (GameCube) packages (*SpongeBob SquarePants: Creature from the Krusty Krab*, `Packages_Rev/` + `AudioRev/`): CRC-keyed index with names matched by CRC; textures (RGBA8 / RGB5A3 / RGB565 / CI4 / CI8 / CMPR / I4 / I8) → PNG; static and soft-skinned actors → GLB (bind pose, no bones). *The House of the Dead: Overkill* `.rev` packages use the same container with a 0x60-byte texture header (RGBA8 / RGB5A3 / CI8 / CMPR, mip chain ignored), also decoded to PNG. *iCarly* (2009) keeps the 0x20-byte resource header in front of a 0x60-byte texture header with explicit palette / pixel offsets (formats 23 RGBA8, 24 RGB5A3, 25/26 CI8 and 27/28 CI4 with RGB565 / RGB5A3 palettes, 29 CMPR, 31 RGB565, 35 IA8; first frame of animated textures) and moves the actor node fields from +0x70 on by 16 bytes, with 0x18-byte batch records {u16 primitive count, ..., u32 texture CRC @0x10}; soft-skinned display lists load a matrix palette (blended envelopes) and prefix each vertex with two matrix slot bytes, positions are already in bind pose. All 4,854 textures and all 927 actors (including the 107 skinned characters, bind pose, no bones) of its 137 asset packages convert |
| **Cars GCT / GCG** | GameCube / Wii / Texture + Model | ✅ | ◐ | Rainbow Studios engine (*Disney-Pixar Cars*; assets ship loose on GameCube, inside U8 `.arc` on Wii): `.gct` CMPR / CI8 textures → PNG (an edited `<name>.png` is re-encoded over the base surface; CI8 against the file's palette; the `.gcg` geometry is not re-encoded); `.gcg` indexed-strip geometry → GLB with the texture named by the material's `.gcm`. Multi-object animation `.gcg` files are recognised but not converted |
| **Konami ZMB** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ⛔ | Konami Bemani engine skinned character model (*Dance Dance Revolution: Winx Club*, `DATA/files/character/CHR*.bin` and `DATA/files/accessory/*.bin`, big-endian). The whole layout below is ground truth from decompiling the loader in the disc's `sys/main.dol` (function `FUN_800cbab4`, found via its `"ZDRAW"`/`"trans"`/bone-name string references), not guessed from data, and independently cross-checked byte-for-byte on retail samples. The file is a single `"WII\0"` wrapper around one or two `"ZMB GC\0\0"` chunks placed back to back (character files: body + head/hair, attached at the body's `mii_head` bone; the second chunk has no wrapper of its own). Chunk header offsets `+0x18`/`+0x1c`/`+0x20` point to a texture-name table (0x20-byte NUL-padded `.tga` names), a material table (count-only decoded so far; records are **not fixed-size** — the loader picks a `0x38` or `0x50` byte stride per whole table from an LOD-switch float) and a bone table. Bone records are a fixed **0xa0 bytes**: name at `+0x00` (matched by the engine against literal bone names, e.g. `"trans"`, `"ZDRAW"`, and a 22-entry known-biped-name table, to find special bones by name rather than index), `+0x2c` a **type** field (`2` = placeholder/no geometry; `4` = a single named anchor point at `+0x70/+0x74/+0x78`; otherwise a 3-row bind-pose transform at `+0x30..+0x6c` and a mesh), submesh count at `+0x9a` and a submesh-array offset at `+0x9c`. Submesh records are a fixed **0x40 bytes** and own four parallel attribute pools (all chunk-relative offsets, present only if non-null): position `f32[3]`/12-byte stride at `+0x24` (count `+0xc`), normal `f32[3]`/12-byte at `+0x2c` (count `+0x14`), UV `f32[2]`/8-byte at `+0x30` (the stored "count" at `+0x18` is unreliable — size from the highest index actually used instead), colour `RGBA8`/4-byte at `+0x34`; `+0x0a` is the vertex-block count and `+0x20` the vertex-block array offset. Vertex-block records are a fixed **0x14 bytes** (0x20 with a per-submesh LOD flag at `+0x04`, adding 3 more optional index arrays not yet decoded): `+0x02` a u16 corner count N (observed 3–7, arbitrary N-gons), then four `u32` offsets to parallel `u32[N]` index arrays into the position/normal/UV/colour pools (`+0x04`/`+0x08`/`+0x0c`/`+0x10`; 0 = attribute absent for this face). Verified end to end: `mii_head_acc08.bin`'s 2 quads decode to two mirrored coplanar quads at ear height with an exact unit-square UV set; `mii_head_acc07.bin`'s 14 N-gon faces (3/4/5/7 corners) reproduce the same geometry found by hand in an earlier pass; `CHR040.bin`'s full 54-bone body exports 2119 vertices / 2901 triangles across both chunks. Bone record `+0x94` (previously read only as a `-1` "no mesh" sentinel) is a complete **parent-bone index array** (`-1` = root; verified against all 54 bones of `CHR040.bin`'s rig by name, e.g. `RightLeg`'s parent is `RightUpLeg`), and the local transform's rotation block at `+0x30..+0x5c` is stored **column-major** (read as rows, a leg chain composes upward off the hip; transposed, it descends correctly to a foot near y=0). Composing local rotation + translation (`+0x60/+0x64/+0x68`) up the parent chain gives each bone a world transform, through which the exported `.obj` now poses every submesh — `CHR040.bin`'s main body mesh (parented near the root) comes out as one coherent, correctly proportioned figure instead of a scattered point cloud. Per-vertex skin weights are decoded but deliberately not applied: `submesh+0x10` is a count equal to that submesh's own position-pool count (one entry per vertex) and `+0x28` a pointer to that many `{influence_count, influence_array}` pairs, each influence a NUL-terminated bone name (`FUN_800b598c`, initially misread as a "closest-bone" match, is a plain `strcmp` resolving the name against every bone) plus an `f32` blend weight at `+0x3c` — verified real normalized skin data on `wb_04_body`'s submesh 0, where multi-influence vertices' weights sum to 1.0 (e.g. 0.333/`Head` + 0.667/`Spine1`). It's a no-op for this decoder's output, though: ZMB carries no animation, so every influence bone's world transform equals its bind transform at the only pose exported, collapsing the skin formula to the same point rigid per-bone attachment already produces — it would only matter for animating the model away from bind pose. Which texture a material binds is now resolved too: one more Gekko-aware decompile (`FUN_800cee48`, the actual texture-bind path, reached from a second render dispatcher `FUN_800ce120`) shows material `+0x18` is a chunk-relative pointer to a small `u32[]` of texture indices — one per customization variant (hair/eye colour swaps pick among them via a per-bone override at `model+0x34`) — with element `[0]` as the model's authored default. Verified against `CHR040.bin` before writing any code: material 2's default slot resolves to texture 2 (`wb_04_body.tga`), material 4's to texture 3 (`wb_04_body_a.tga`) — exactly the assignment implied by the material-index work below. Submesh `+0x00` is confirmed as a material index — verified on `CHR040.bin`'s `wb_04_body` bone, whose 3 submeshes use materials 2/3/4 of that chunk's 5, no repeats or gaps — so the exported `.obj`'s `usemtl` groups now reference a generated `.mtl` with a real `map_Kd` per resolvable material (the referenced PNG is named after the on-disk `.tga` stem; ZMB itself doesn't decode texture pixels, so extracting the sibling texture-pack file to match is left to the caller). A character's second (head/hair) chunk is now re-rooted under the body chunk's `mii_head` bone before posing, so both chunks assemble as one figure — verified on `CHR040.bin`: the head mesh lands right on top of the body mesh's peak instead of floating in its own disconnected local space. `lib-zmb.c`/`.h` implement `IsZMB()`/`DecodeZMB()`, wired into `GetByMagicFF()` (structural detection, since `.bin` is shared with MPBIN/ATB/etc.) and `wszst EXTRACT`: writes a sibling `.zmb.yaml` (textures, material count, full bone table) and a sibling `.obj` (real fan-triangulated geometry with position/normal/UV, one group per bone/submesh, in each submesh's own local-pool space). Ghidra repro: `main.dol`'s 18 DOL segments flattened to one image at base `0x80004000`, language `PowerPC:BE:32:default`, loader function is the one function (of the 3 total) that cross-references the literal bone-name strings `"trans"`, `"ZDRAW"` and the 22-entry known-bone-name table |

| **SEC** | Wii / Scene | ✅ | ❌ | *Octomania* "secB" scene/demo container (`.sec`): confirmed header + 32-byte chunk table with resolved names via a shared string table; the payload is a run of standard NW4R files on a 0x800 grid after the table (`RSAR`/`RSTM`/`RWSD`... sound files in `snddemo.sec`, `bres` in `g3ddemo.sec`, layouts in `lytdemo.sec`); `wszst x` cuts out every plain member as `NNN.brsar` / `.brstm` / `.brres` ... (checked: sizes match the embedded headers, extracted BRRES parse). About half of the members are stored packed with a token stream (b < 0x80 = b literals, b >= 0x80 = (b & 0x7f) + 1 copies of the next byte); that reproduces the NW4R headers but the sheet data of packed RFNTs comes out ~100 KB short, so packed members are skipped. |
| **WT** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | *Kidz Sports Basketball* / *London Taxi* / *Battle Rage* ... `sound/roland/gm16pcm.wt` + `.pcm` (the console form of `GM16.DLS`): BE `u32` section offsets `{0x18, ..., size}`; the last section holds 16-byte records `{rate (low 16 bits), start, length (samples), 0}` into the companion BE s16 `.pcm`. Each of the 495 samples is written as `wave_NNNN.wav` (checked against the DLS waves: identical data); the `...adpcm` variant decodes too (sample starts in nibbles, DSP-ADPCM frames, 16 coefficients per sample in a 46-byte-per-sample trailer after the last section; matches the PCM waves to within ADPCM quantisation error); the instrument / key maps are written as `map.json` (checked on `gm16adpcm.wt`): section 0 = drum key maps (128 rows x 128 keys of u16 region ids, `0xffff` = none; the non-empty rows 0, 8, 16, 24, 25, 32, 40, 48, 56 are the GS drum kits), section 1 = melodic maps (128 programs x 128 keys of region ids), section 2 = 24-byte regions `{root key, tune, volume, u16 ?, loop start, loop length, layer, wave}` (for all 1114 looped regions loop start + loop length + 1 equals the wave's length); the meaning of the u16 field, of `tune` / `volume` units and of section 3 (60880 bytes) is not identified; `tests/mk_octowt.py` (PASS) |

The inner level / graphics layouts are not decoded. |

BFSAR/BCSAR/SDAT still use the external vgmtrans CLI |

Only synthetic SDATs were available to test with |

Wave names (RWSD) are not read |
| NW4R instrument banks (Wii) | `.brbnk` / `.rbnk` | `xx` writes the wrbnk XML dump and, when a `.brwar` of the same name is next to the bank, a SoundFont of it |

| **Kylotonn `.snd`** (Cocoto Festival, Wii) | `wszst xx x.snd` | Little-endian `NAME/FREQ/STER/SIZE/CSZE/SAMP` chunks; SAMP is a 16-bit-word-swapped standard DSP header + ADPCM. Swapped back and written as `ch0.dsp`/`ch0.wav` (181/181 files in the game decode). `.txc` (BTCH/MTRD; PACK 5 = Huffman-coded LZ, `gx_crunch_pack5`) decodes: 32 bit ARGB and 16 bit ARGB4444 images to PNG (277/277 in the game); 8 bit images (paletted, 211) are written raw as `.8bpp` + `.pal` because their pixel layout is not understood. `.mtd` (UI bitmaps: name + SIZX/SIZY/BITP 24/PACK 5/PICT) -> PNG, 195/195. `.mdl` (MODL blocks VERT/FACE/PFCS, PACK 3 = LZ, PACK 5 = Huffman LZ) exports geometry to `<name>.glb`: 226/226 models, polygons fan-triangulated, UVs (texel units / 256), per-face materials; the 16/32 bit `textures_wii/<name>.txc` of each material is written as `<name>.png` beside the GLB and embedded (8 bit textures are not), no transforms yet. |
| **AST (Proteus `SDASSETF`)** | Wii / Container + Texture | ✅ | ❌ | Proteus-engine asset bundles (`.ast`; *I Spy Spooky Mansion*, *Goosebumps HorrorLand*, *Spore Hero*, ...). Header `SDASSETF` {u32 version 1, u32 chunk count}; each top-level chunk is `{tag, version, fixed size, children size}` + NUL-terminated name padded to 4 + fixed payload + child chunks `{tag, version, field, size}`. All 1598 `.ast` files of *Goosebumps HorrorLand* all 190 of *I Spy Spooky Mansion* all 126 of *Ultimate I Spy* all 303 of *I Spy Game Pack* and 709 of *National Geographic Challenge!* (417 of 419 models, 1436 textures; extra tags NDAN, NDST, NDOU, AGPH; three 16-byte empty bundles) (4450 of 4634 models, 3017 textures) (2093 of 2102 models and 1287 textures export; extra scene tags IMDL, ILGT, IMSD, IFOG) (151 big-endian, 39 little-endian; 2062 models and 1716 textures export without errors, textures checked visually) (832 big-endian, 766 little-endian/word-swapped `SADSFTES`) walk to EOF. Top-level tags (also ALST, ATRE, POLY in Spooky Mansion; `REF@` model instances carry one float that their size fields do not count): BMAP (IMAG, CLUT), MTRL (EFCT), MDL (BND, INFO, LOD with MESH/WDGE/DATA), SKEL, SPLM (SPCH), LGHT, PRSP, FRCE, PLNE. `xx` writes the structure to `<file>.d/<file>.txt`; for big-endian bundles each BMAP also decodes to `<name>_<n>.png` (BMAP fixed payload: u16 width/height twice, log2 sizes at +12, format byte at +15 (7 = CMPR), index bits at +16 (32 = RGBA8, 4/8 = C4/C8 with an RGB5A3 CLUT child); 6028 PNGs from the Goosebumps disc, checked visually). Models: every `MDL` chunk (all of them live in the little-endian bundles) exports to `<name>.glb` -- children DATA (u32 header {.., .., stride, count, component flags}, then interleaved vertices; flag bytes 1 position, 2 normal, 3 RGBA8 colour, 4 UV, 0x80 marker), WDGE {vertex count, index count, u32 indices; a triangle list when the MESH triangle count x 3 equals the index count, otherwise a strip with degenerate joints}, MESH {name, bounds, {material field, index count, 1, 0, 0, triangle count}} paired by the child field id; MTRL `EFCT` child names the texture (`\\Proteus\\TEXTURED` effect, texture name at +24), which is decoded from a sibling big-endian bundle and written next to the GLB; 5299 of 5470 Goosebumps models export (bounds match the MESH bounds exactly), meshes without a WDGE chunk (baked-lighting models) read their indices from the MESH tail (index count, then one entry per index repeated per vertex component, bytes for up to 256 vertices, 16 bit above). The remaining ~4% are skinned characters whose WDGE refers to more wedges than the DATA block holds vertices (wedge to vertex mapping not understood; WGHT chunk holds the skin weights). Not decoded: the 22 little-endian textures (different layout), 640x480 tiled screens (named tile grid, no IMAG), skin weights/bones (MESH tail), LOD and INFO contents. |

| **Radical RCF (ATG)** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | 32-byte `"ATG CORE CEMENT LIBRARY"`, version `02 01 01 01`, then big-endian `table_off (0x3c)`, `table_end_first_part`, `names_off`, `names_len`, `0`, `count`. Table at `0x3c`: `count * {u32 name_hash, u32 offset, u32 size}` sorted by hash, offsets multiples of `0x800`. Names block at `names_off`: `{LE 0x800, LE 0}` then, in ascending **file-offset** order and little-endian, `{u32 mtime, u32 0x800, u32 0, u32 name_len, name (backslash paths, NUL included), 3 zero bytes}`; the i-th name belongs to the i-th smallest offset (checked: `.p3d` -> `P3D\xff`, `.blua` -> `\x1bLua`, `.rsd` -> `RSD6`, `.bik` -> `BIKi`, `.bfig` -> `fig0`, in 100% of members). The name-hash function is not identified and is not needed. Some `sound.rcf` P3D chunks are byte-swapped (`\xffD3P`). Verified on Crash of the Titans: 7/7 archives tile to the exact file length; `default.rcf` 1851/1851 members byte-identical after extraction. |
| **NIBM (.aud)** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `NIBM`, `u32 2`, `u32 15`, length-prefixed type names `class AudioData` / `struct AudioData::Streamed`, then a few fields (44100 or 22050 Hz appear) and a complete Ogg Vorbis stream to end of file. Detection: magic + first `OggS` within 4 KiB + Ogg page walk that must tile the file exactly with at least 3 pages. Verified: 311/311 files of *CSI: Hard Evidence* (Ogg at offset 126 in each; 22050 Hz mono SFX up to 3 min 44.1 kHz stereo music decode cleanly). Other games using the `.aud` extension (FIFA, Big Beach Sports 2, Destroy All Humans) are different formats and are declined by the magic check. |
| **Harmonix Ark** | Wii/PS2/360 / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | `main.hdr` + `main_0.ark`, `main_1.ark`, ... . Header: if the first u32 is not a known version (2-7, 9, 10) it is a key and every following byte is XORed with the low byte of a Park-Miller generator stepped once per byte (`k = (k - (k/127773)*127773)*16807 - (k/127773)*2836`, `+0x7fffffff` if <= 0); a second failure means the version is stored complemented and the rest needs another `^0xff`. Decrypted (little-endian even on Wii): `version`, [v>=6: `n` + 16-byte hashes], `part_count`, `size_count`, part sizes (u32; u64 in v4, with a "broken v4" fallback when the last one exceeds 32 bits), [v>=5: part path strings], [6..9: 4-byte hashes], [v>=7: file collections], then for v<=7 a NUL-string blob + index table + entries `{offset (i64 for v>=4), file idx, dir idx, size, inflated size}`; v>=9 stores full path strings. Offsets are global across the parts. Member paths like `../../system/run/...` are folded to `system/run/...` (82 of 3418 on the AC/DC disc). Verified on real data: v4 AC/DC Live Track Pack (all 3418 members byte-identical to the ark, sizes sum to the ark length exactly) and v5 Beatles Rock Band (7577 members, sizes sum to the ten listed parts exactly; parts not extracted); v6+ and inflated members are ported from Mackiloha but untested here. |
| **XPK** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | Exient `.pak` (*Angry Birds Star Wars*), all big-endian: `u32 0x58504b01`, `u32 version` (2/3/4/9 seen, same layout), `u32 files` (file entries only), `u32 names_size`; 32-byte entries from `0x50`: `{0, name_off, size, offset, flags, mtime, csize, 0}`. `flags` 1 = zlib (`csize` bytes at `offset`), 0 = stored (`size` bytes, csize 0). Directory records (`size` 0) come first and give `{offset = first child entry index, csize = child count}`; entries outside any range are root files. The entry count is not stored: the first file's data begins exactly where the name table ends, so `entries = (min file offset - names_size - 0x50) / 32`, which is also the geometry check. Verified: 29/322/409/30 entries in the scripts/levels/images/extras packs, all zlib members inflate to their exact size, entry counts equal the header counts once directory rows are added. Members are Lua scripts, `.xgt` textures and `.dat` sprite sheets (not decoded here). |
| **FEZC package (`.fez`)** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | WiiWare GVF titles such as *Yard Sale Hidden Treasures*. Little-endian: `FEZC`, version `0x10000`, count `N` at +8, name table at `0x28` (`u32` padded length + NUL-terminated path per member), then `N` × 32-byte records `{flags (0x102 = sync-flushed zlib, 0 = stored), offset, csize, usize, MD5}`, data after the records. All 3,345 members of the sample inflate to their recorded size; JPEG, OGG and PNG members have valid end markers. |
| **Artefacts MAP / GAM** | Wii / Level database | 🟡 | ❌ | Same `FAAFFAAF` stream as the `.cfg/.gam/.loc` files of *Diabolik* (see `lib-diabolik.h`), big-endian: `u32 0xFAAFFAAF, u32 8`, then one outermost section: `u32 0xBBBBBBBB`, `u32 end` (file offset of the matching `0xBEBEBEBE` closing tag), `u32 version`, payload (raw fields plus nested sections, sections 4-aligned), closing tag; file ends `0xFEEFFEEF`. Recovered from the game's own reader in `main.dol` (`0xFBBFFBBF` is the byte-swapped twin). Objects are polymorphic (class id plus a section written by that class' serializer), so field meaning depends on the class. Textures: name section (`u32 20` + 20 ASCII bytes), an 8-byte record `fmt, 0, 1, u16 width, u16 height, 0`, then a leaf section `u8 1, u32 mips, u32 size, u32 0` + GX CMPR mip chain (largest first, min one 8x8 tile per level) + 3 pad bytes; chain sizes matched on all 304 textures of the 25 *Boot Camp Academy* maps. Skinned meshes (`FUN_800f90a8`/`FUN_800f8a3c` in the game): leaf `u8 1`, `u32 V` + V x 16-byte vertices (`s16 x,y,z` /4096, Z up, model space; `u8 bone0, bone1`; `s8 nx,ny,nz` /64; 5 skin bytes), `u32 T` + T x `u16 u,v` /1024, `u32 P` + P x 6-byte positions and `u32 C` + C x 4 bytes (the shadow/outline pass), then 32 slots of `u8 n` x {`u16 first, u16 count, u32 tris, u16, u32 dlsize`, display list, 8 bytes bone palette}, `u8 n` x {same without the palette}, `u8 n` x {`u32 k, u32`, k x 8 bytes}. The first kind's display lists are `0x98` strips (`0x00` NOPs) of `{u16 pos, u16 nrm, u16 tex}` vertices, position/normal relative to `first`; first/count tile V exactly and the triangle counts match (drill sergeant: 1883 vertices, 3479 strip triangles, 5 slots). Objects and references: the game keeps a class table (`FUN_80006960(…, id, parent, factory, …)` registers ~430 classes; named groups include Textures `0xa`, Meshes `0xe`, Materials `0x10`, MaterialSets `0x23`, Animations `0x2e`, Skins `0x45`, Maps `0x65`); an object reference in the stream is `s16 class, s16 n, u64 uid, [u32 size if n>0], u8 flag`, flag 1 meaning the object's body section follows immediately (otherwise it names an object defined earlier, possibly in the shared `.gam`). A model object holds a material set (class `0x25`: `u32 count` + count references, in slot order); slot *i* of the mesh uses entry *i*. A material (classes `0x49`/`0x1b`) holds its textures as class-12 references, the first being the diffuse map; a texture body is the object described above. `.gam` (class `0x64`, 7 MB in *Boot Camp Academy*) is the same stream. Skeleton: the mesh object (class `0x1c`, `FUN_800bf004`, version 6-8) holds, after its base section, five `u16`, then `u32 n` + n x 80-byte bones `{s32 parent, f32 pos[3], f32 rot[3][3], name28}` (parents first; local rotation is the transpose of the stored matrix, world = parent * [R^T | pos]; Z up, root `dm_*`), then `u32 m` + m x 48-byte inverse bind transforms `{f32 t[3], f32 R[3][3]}` with `local = R^T p + t`. Vertex bone *k* (u8 at +6, +7 and +11) is entry *k* of that table and belongs to skeleton bone *k+1*; weights are the u8s at +12..+14 (all `0xff` or all zero = rigid). Posing the exported skeleton moves arms and head cleanly with no tearing. Animations (class `0x2e`, loader `FUN_800b6e30`, sampler `FUN_800b7ce0`; the object is a named base section followed by one raw gap): `u32 ms_per_frame` (20), `u32 duration_ms`, `u16 frames`, `u16 bones`, 4 flag bytes, `u32 nRot, nTrans, nScale`, `f32 transScale` (2^-12), `f32 scaleScale`, one flag byte per bone (`0x01` rotation constant, `0x02` translation constant, `0x04` scale constant, `0x08` default scale, `0x20`/`0x40`/`0x80` no rotation/translation/scale channel), then the pools `nRot x 4 x s16` quaternions (x, y, z, w) /16384, `nTrans x 3 x s16` translations x `transScale` (metres, Z up) and `nScale x 3 x s16`, then three tables each `u32 n` + n x `u16`: rotation, translation and scale key indices. Per bone with a non-constant channel the table holds one index per frame into the pool (a constant channel has one entry); an empty table means the pool holds one entry per frame directly. The local rotation matrix is the transpose of the quaternion's matrix, so glTF gets (-x, -z, y, w) after the Z-up to Y-up change. Frame 0 translations equal the skeleton's bone positions and constant rotations its stored matrices; the pool sizes are exactly what the flags predict for all 15 sergeant clips. The drill sergeant's *drink* clip renders as a coherent sequence (gesturing, then raising a hand to the mouth with the head tilted back). Scale channels have not been seen are not decoded. Static meshes (class `0x14`, `FUN_800db7c8`/`FUN_80107cf8`): leaf `u8 1`, `u32 NP` + NP x s16 x3, `u32 NU` + NU x `u16 u,v` /1024, `u32 ND` + ND x `{u16 slot, u32 tris, u32 size, display list}`; the display lists are `0x99` strips (`{u16 pos, u8 colour, u16 normal, u16 tex}` vertices; the `0x98` form has three u16) with the slot's low 15 bits selecting the material; positions are divided by the f32 "units per metre" stored after the radius in the mesh's base section (`4096` for the character, `128` for the stage backdrop). The leaf parses to within its alignment padding on the *Boot Camp Academy* backdrop (49 lists, 12153 strip triangles equal to the stored counts). |
| **Blue Castle BIG** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *The Bigs* / *The Bigs 2* `.big`, all little-endian: `u32 0x01020304`, `u32 data_start`, `u32 file_size` (== real size), `u32 count`, `u32 table (0x18)`, `u32 names`; 20-byte entries `{name_off, size, offset, kind, 0}` (`kind` 4/256 nested archive, 32 model blob, 2048 aligned audio; never compressed); NUL-separated names at `names`. Audio members `.dspi` hold `N` identical-shaped 0x60-byte DSP headers followed by the `N` channel bodies, each padded to 8 bytes (last unpadded); the total size rule `0x60*N + N*pad8(ceil(nibbles/2))` matched all 4264 members (3708 stereo, 556 mono). Announcer `.pcm` and `.sfx`/`.bcd` members are not decoded. |
| **Natsume BIN** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Harvest Moon* `.bin`, little-endian: `"BIN\0"`, `u16 1`, `u16 1`, `u32 count`, `u32 0`, then `count` x `{u32 offset, u32 size, u32 0, u32 0}` from 0x10. Members plain. |
| **Town Factory PCKG** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Little King's Story*, big-endian: `"PCKG"` + 28 zero bytes, then from 0x20 entries `{u32 next (0 = last), u32 size, u32 data_offset (0x20), char[20] name, payload}`; entries padded to 0x20. |
| **Neversoft GH PAK / IMG** | Wii / Archive | ✅ | ❌ | *Guitar Hero* (Wii) `.pak.ngc`, big-endian: 0x20-byte headers `{u32 type crc, u32 offset, u32 size, u32 crc, 0, u32 name crc, 0, u32 flags}` (flag 0x20 = 160-byte path follows), ended by an all-zero type/offset/size header. `.img.ngc`: `04 20`, log2 w/h at 0x0a/0x0b, GX format at 0x0d (14 = CMPR), size at 0x10, data at 0x20. |
| **WSI** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | `u32 start (0x20)`, `u32 channels (2)`, then block sets of one block per channel (channel 1 first): `u32 size, u32 1, u32 channel (1-based), u32 0` + payload. The first block of each channel starts its payload with a standard 0x60-byte DSP header (nsamples, nibbles, rate, loop, coefficients, history); the rest is ADPCM frames. Concatenating each channel's payloads gives a plain `.dsp`. A chain-walk probe (equal block sizes per set, alternating channel numbers, blocks tiling the file exactly) gates the claim. Verified on the *Aladdin Magic Racer* streams (decoded sample count equals the header's, audio smooth: mean\|dx\|/mean\|x\| ≈ 0.24). |
| **DSi TAD (.tad)** | DSi / Title package | ✅ | ❌ | Wii-WAD-style container: `0x20` header (`u32 0x20`, type `"Is"`, `u16 ver`, then BE sizes of cert chain, CRL, ticket, TMD, data, footer), each section padded to `0x40`. Ticket body follows a `0x140`-byte RSA-2048 signature block: wrapped title key at body+`0x7f`, title ID at +`0x9c`. TMD body: title ID +`0x4c`, content count +`0x9e`, boot index +`0xa0`, **`0x24`-byte** content records `{u32 id, u16 index, u16 type, u64 size, sha1[20]}` from +`0xa4`. Title key = AES-128-CBC-decrypt(wrapped, DSi common key, IV = title ID + 8 zeros); content = AES-128-CBC(title key, IV = `{index BE u16, 14 zeros}`) rounded up to 16. The right common key (retail `AF1BF516…`, debug `A2FDDDF2…`, Wii debug `A1604A6A…`) is found by matching the decrypted content's SHA-1 to the TMD, so a wrong guess is never emitted. Verified end to end on a real DSiWare title (real TMD/content/title key from the No-Intro CDN set; the ticket was forged since none is distributed). Signatures unchecked; encrypt/repack not implemented. |
| **DSiWare export (.bin)** | DSi / Title package | ✅ | ❌ | SD-card export per GBATEK/DSiBrew, every part an ES block (data + `0x20` metablock: 16-byte CCM MAC + 16 bytes holding marker `0x3a`, size and the nonce): banner `0x0000`+`0x4000`, header `0x4020`+`0xb4` (`"4ANT"`, sizes at `0x28`: tmd, app, 7 unused, public.sav, banner.sav), footer `0x40f4`+`0x440` (SHA-1s at `0x00/0x14/0x28/0x3c…0xdc/0xf0`, AP cert `0x140`, TW cert `0x2c0` whose key name ends in the 16-hex-digit ConsoleID), then the parts back to back at `0x4554`. Fixed key `3DA3EA33…`; console key = `F_XY(keyX = 4E00004A 4A00004E (id_lo ^ C80C4B72) id_hi, keyY = CCFCA703…)` with `F_XY(x,y) = ((C + (x^y)) <<< 42)`, `C = 1A4F3E79 2A680F5F 29590258 FFFEFB4E`, all little-endian; each part is tried under both keys and the MAC decides. Crypto cross-checked against a build of WinterMute/twltool (fixed and console-key blocks decrypt identically); no retail SD dump was available. |
| **DSi modcrypt** | DSi / ROM layer | ✅ | ❌ | AES-CTR over up to two SRL regions (`0x220/0x224`, `0x228/0x22c`), active when header `0x1c` bit 1 is set, IVs at `0x300` and `0x314`. Retail key = `F_XY("Nintendo" + gamecode + gamecode reversed, header[0x350..])`, debug (`0x1c` bit 2 or `0x1bf` bit 7) = header[0..16]. The counter and keystream are byte-reversed relative to standard CTR. Output clears the flag and rewrites the CRC16 at `0x15e` when it was valid before, since twltool's flag-preserving output would re-encrypt on a second pass. Verified on a real title: both regions go from ~7.99 to ~5.9 bits/byte entropy, header CRC valid before and after. |

| **IOI Glacier texture bank** | Wii / Texture | ✅ | ◐ | Mini Ninjas `.TEX` (one per scene `.zip`): big-endian header `{table offset, end, 0, 0x04000000}`, then 16-byte aligned entries `{u32 size, format x2, u32 id, u16 width, u16 height, u32 mips, 0, f32 1.0, 0, name\0, mips x {u32 size, data}}` (`PALN` adds `{u32 count, count x ABGR}`; entries whose two format words differ are id groups `{count, ids}`). Pixels are linear, not GX-tiled: `DXT1` = PC DXT1 with big-endian colour words, `15A3` RGB5A3, `16A0` RGB565, `IA4`, `IA8` (alpha first), `I4`, `I8`, `RGBA` and palettes stored A,B,G,R, `PALN` 8-bit indices. An edited PNG (at any depth below `<file>.d/`, named as decoded) is re-encoded over the base level in place (DXT1 / 15A3 / 16A0 / IA4 / IA8 / I4 / I8 / RGBA / PALN against the entry's palette; mip levels keep the old picture). The base level is written as `<file>.d/<name>.png` (unnamed: `tex_<id>.png`). All 12,367 textures of the 59 retail banks decode. The packs themselves are ordinary zips with two quirks now accepted by the ZIP reader: a 22-byte `enuR` record after the end-of-central-directory record and 4 bytes after each deflate stream (11 of 299 packs); `tests/mk_glacier_tex.py` (PASS) |
| **IOI Glacier sound bank** | Wii / Audio | ✅ | ❌ | Mini Ninjas scene sound banks: `<scene>.WHD` (records) beside `<scene>.WAV` (data), both starting `{u32, u32 file size, 0x03000000, 0x04000000}`. Records are big-endian `{u16 type (1 PCM, 0x76 DSP-ADPCM), u16 flags (bit 15 = streamed from `Wii_*.str`), rate, format (0x10 PCM16, 4 DSP), decoded bytes, data size, channels, offset in the .WAV, samples (all channels), ...}`; a DSP record is preceded by `align8(46 * channels) + 8` bytes of 46-byte per-channel blocks `{32 bytes of coefficients, gain, predictor/scale, hist1, hist2, 6 pad}`; stereo data is two consecutive halves. Every stored sound is written as `<file>.WHD.d/NNNN.wav` (no names in the bank). All 13,311 stored sounds of the 59 retail banks decode (13,100 mono and 142 stereo DSP, 69 PCM), and every record's predictor/scale equals the first frame header of its channel; the 10,638 streamed records live in the `.str` banks (see IOISNDSTREAM). Header-only loader banks are accepted silently; `tests/mk_glacier_whd.py` (PASS) |
| **IOI Glacier localisation** | Wii / Text | ✅ | ❌ | Mini Ninjas `<scene><Language>.LOC` (in the scene `.zip` packs): a name tree. The root is `{u8 count, (count - 1) x u32 LE offset of child i relative to child 0}`; each child is `name\0` + type byte: `0x10` group (same count/offset form, count 0 = empty), `0x09` UTF-8 string `text\0` + u32 0, `0x29` dialogue string with `text\0` + u32 id twice, `0x08` name only. Written as `<file>.d/strings.tsv` (`path<TAB>text[<TAB>id]`). All 138 retail files walk to EOF with every offset matching: 96,586 strings; `tests/mk_glacier_loc.py` (PASS) |
| **IOI Glacier model container** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | Mini Ninjas `.prm` (in the scene `.zip` packs): big-endian `{table offset, chunk count, table offset, 0}` header, chunk table `{offset, size, u32, 0}` at the end of the file. Geometry is reached through 16-byte mesh descriptors `{u32 vertex count, u32 vertex buffer chunk, u16 material, u16 flags, u32 index buffer chunk}`; index buffers are `{u16 1, u16 count, u16 indices}` triangle lists, vertices are 20 bytes `{f32 x, y, z, u8 0, s8 normal xyz /127, s16 u, v /512}` (UV unit confirmed by 21,770 meshes spanning exactly 0..512; flags-0 buffers carry 2 more bytes per vertex after the array). Descriptors are accepted only when the largest index is vertex count - 1. Each mesh becomes one object (`mesh_<chunk>_m<material>`) in `<file>.d/model.obj`: 36,325 meshes in the 23 retail files with geometry. Meshes are in object space; instance transforms (scene `.buf`/`.gms`), materials (`.MAT`), skinning and the other chunk types are not decoded yet; `tests/mk_glacier_prm.py` (PASS) |
| **Sumo COG/GOG** | Wii / Model + Texture | ✅ | ❌ | Virtua Tennis 2009 `.cog` + `.gog`: `*.TGA` texture entries (CMPR/IA8, pixels in the `.gog`) decode to PNG (checked on a racket texture); meshes with float position pools and indexed GX display lists (XF matrix loads skipped) export as `.obj` (positions, `/512` UVs, `/64` normals), one group per mesh in joint-local space (no node transforms or skinning). Of all 2,671 `.cog` files on the disc, 272 yield meshes; of the rest, 2,100 contain no position descriptor at all (animation/scene data) and 300 have no `.gog`, skinned meshes (extra leading matrix-index bytes per vertex, found by trying 0-2 extra bytes) are included; secondary draw lists with other vertex layouts (e.g. in `Trophy_Shield`) are undecoded; `.gpu` is not handled.
| **SEGA GRO3** | Wii / Model | ✅ | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.gro3` (inside `.urs`, extracted by `xx`): chunk container whose KFMD mesh data is a PS2-style VIF packet stream; each V4-32 / V3-32 / S-8 / V2-16 UNPACK quadruple (position + flag, s32 normal /32768, texcoord /4096) is exported as a triangle strip group of an `.obj` in model space (checked on a unit model: 971 strips, unit-length normals, symmetric 38-unit figure). The `.hmdl` members of `.amf`/`.urs` archives wrap the same stream and use the same exporter (models without normals or texcoords, plain V4-32 position triples, become one triangle each; a character and its LOD pair come out at the same 10 x 15.7 extent). Bone/skin data, textures and the material table are not decoded |
| **SEGA HMOT** | Wii / Animation | 🟡 | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.hmot` (inside `.amf`/`.urs`; layout recovered from 73 retail motions, all parse): `HMOT` header, then a `KFMO` chunk (offsets relative to 0x20) `{u32 flags, u16 bones, u16 1, f32 frames, u32 bone table offset}`; a bone entry is 16 bytes `{0, mask << 24, offset of the curve offset list, offset of the base values}`; channels are the set mask bits from bit 0 up (0-3 rotation components, 5-7 translation x y z), one f32 base value and one u32 curve offset (0 = static) each. A curve is `{u8 1, u8 bytes per sample, u8 binary point, 0, 8 zero bytes, u32 sample offset}` and, with 2 bytes per sample, frames + 1 s16 values / 2^binary point (smooth cycles such as a leg swing). Written as `<name>.json` with base values and curves. How the curve values combine with the base values, and the rare 3-byte curves (all zero in the checked files), are not verified. |
| **SEGA ABST** | Wii / Data | 🟡 | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.asd` asset schedules (all 47 retail files parse): `{"ABST", u32 size, u32 header size, 0, ...}` with a zero-terminated list of chunk offsets from 0x24; chunks are `{tag, u32, u32, 0}`: `ASAD` asset descriptors (32-byte name at +0x10: `evmap05_01_00`, `light_ev05_00`, `fog05_00_01`, `GZR_EFEV0504_C01`, `glare_ev05_00`, ...) and `ASSC` schedule blocks. Written as `<name>.json` with tag, offset, size, name and the payload u32 words; the numeric fields are not interpreted. |
| **SEGA GLIP** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.cnk` lip-sync tables (all 41 retail files parse): `{"GLIP", u32 size, u32 header size 0x10, 0}`, `u32 entry count, u32 8`, then count entries `{u32 key, u32 record offset}` (keys ascending: `0x16000 + n`, later voice ids such as `0x1f7ee`; offsets relative to 0x10, a record runs to the next offset). A record byte is `{mouth pattern : 3 bits, frames : 5 bits}`, the pattern held for that many frames, pattern 6 ending the record (lip-sync stepper `FUN_8006f7e4` in `main.dol`; the pattern 0-5 becomes the face's mouth, 6 -> -1); all 8848 retail records end that way. Written as `<name>.json` with key, record bytes in hex and the decoded `[pattern, frames]` keys |
| **SEGA MSCR** | Wii / Data | 🟡 | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.msX` compressed script binaries (all 43 retail files unpack): `{"MSCR", u32 payload size, u32 header size 0x20 (0x10 in two files, no hash), u32 flags (0x400 = compressed), 16-byte hash}`, then `u32 unpacked size, u32 0x8000, 8 x 0xff` and the stream at header size + 0x10. The stream is an LZ with every byte and flag bit inverted (found in the game's "Melt" decoder): flags LSB first; 1 = literal; `01` = long match `{b0, b1, (b2)}` (length `b0 & 7`, 0 means `b2 + 1`, else `+ 2`; offset `b1 * 0x20 + (b0 >> 3) - 0x2000`; both zero ends); `00` = short match (2 flag bits = length - 2, one byte, offset - 0x100); 8 KiB window. Written as the plain `.msb` (header with flags cleared + unpacked body, same layout as the uncompressed `shoptrial.msb`). The bytecode itself is not decoded. |
| **SEGA MSB** | Wii / Data | 🟡 | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love unpacked `.msb` script binaries (`shoptrial.msb` and every unpacked `.msX`, 44 files parse): after the MSCR header the body starts with 8 words `{hash, hash, word table offset, word count, entry table offset (0x20, or 0x18 with the 0x10 byte header), entry count, 0, 0}`; an entry is `{u32 id, u32 argument block offset, u32 argument word count, u32 code offset}` (offsets relative to the body), the code is a stream of 16 bit instruction words running to the next code offset (last: to the word table). Written as `<name>.json` with the word table, per entry id, arguments and code bytes in hex; each entry also carries `asm`, a token listing that names the code words with the game's own instruction table (337 opcodes with stack argument counts read from main.dol: core `0x15..0x77` variable access / jump / if / call / return / operators / threads, Plane/Effect/Action/Camera modules `0x1000..0x2f81`, `slg*` battle calls `0x4000..0x4220`) and prints bit-15 words as operands (`#8002`); the stream is a stack machine (`PlaneSearch` is preceded by its four pushed operands) but the inline-operand and stack semantics are not decoded, so it is a token stream, not a verified disassembly. |
| **SEGA GHSL** | Wii / Data | ✅ | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.bin` token trees (event/area/move tables; all 21 retail files; header flag `0x200` (only `IF/ECATCH/gameinfo.bin`, the battle and camera balance table) marks an encrypted body: each byte goes through one of three 256-byte substitution tables (`i % 3`, from `main.dol` 0x803df7f0), then from the end `b[i] -= b[i-1] + 0x7f + i`, as `FUN_8000e39c` does at load): `{"GHSL", u32 size, u32 0x10, u32 flags}`, u32 distance to the string table at 0x10 (`u32 count`, offsets, Shift-JIS strings), token stream at 0x14. Token kinds by the top two bits: `00` node (byte counts of name index / child offset / sibling offset), `01` attribute (name index, value, type 0 number / 2 string), `10` plain value, `11` end of children. Written as `<name>.json` tree `{name, attrs, children}` with UTF-8 strings. |

Each cell's textured quads are decoded into the JSON (96-byte records `{u32 state, u32 state, f32 centre x, y, size, f32 0, 4 x {f32 x, y, u, v}, 2 x f32 0}`, canvas pixel coordinates and 0..1 UVs into the 256x256 atlas, 64 per cell in the sample); how the state words select one of the cell's textures is not decoded. | **SEGA GTPA** | Wii / Text | 🟡 | ❌ | Sakura Wars: So Long, My Love `.gtp` and some `.arm` text page tables (all 17 retail files parse): `{"GTPA", u32 size, u32 header size 0x10, 0}`, `u32 record count`, count u32 record offsets (relative to 0x10). | **Manhunt 2 world and model geometry (WRLD / CLMP)** | Wii / World | 🟡 | ❌ | *Manhunt 2* `.bsp` worlds and `.dff` models (inside MH2Z, characters are plain CLMP): each mesh sector starts with the marker `00b749e0`; at +0x38 comes a stream `{u16 count, u8 format<<4 \| shift, u8 components, vertices}` (`f32` xyz for props and worlds, `s16` x 3 or 6 components with the binary point in the format byte for characters; the vertex data starts at sector start + 0x1c + the u32 stored just before the stream header, which is always 8 bytes after the count), followed by 16-byte table entries `{u32 offset, u32 size, 0x01000000, 0x150100nn}` locating its GX display lists (display list = sector start + offset + 0x1c; draws 0x80/0x90/0x98/0xa0; vertices of constant size, the first u16 being a position index). Written as `.obj`: one `o sector…` group per sector with positions and faces (`A04_SM_Nightclub/scene1.bsp`: 22579 faces; Leo's full body decodes recognisably as a T-pose (the whole body decodes with no gaps: an earlier version clamped each draw to 4096 vertices and dropped about 150 triangles of a 4548-vertex draw. Display list vertices are laid out by the vertex descriptor below: a 9-byte character vertex is u16 position index, u16 normal index, u8 colour index, u16 UV 0 index, u16 UV 1 index), plus the auxiliary 16-byte `{f32 x, y, z, 0}` buffers as `o buffer…` groups. Table entries are either marked (`01000000 150100nn`) or plain `{offset, size, 0, 0}`. Character streams carry 6 components (position and a unit normal, both s16 with 14 fractional bits); the normals are written as `vn` and faces as `v//vn`. UVs are written as `vt` (`s16` pairs, 11 fractional bits; the UV stream and the byte offset of the UV index inside the display list vertices are chosen by the smallest mean UV distance between consecutive vertices, so they are a heuristic, and sectors without a fitting stream get none). The mesh record that points at the sector (u32 at record + 108) starts with the texture table (u32 pointer, u32 count; 12-byte entries beginning with a name pointer). A display list's material index is the u16 in the two bytes before its table entry (the `nn` of the previous entry's `150100nn`; 0 for the first entry) and the byte before that is its bone id, so each display list gets a `usemtl <texture>` and a `.mtl` with `map_Kd <texture>.png` is written beside the `.obj`. The two bytes before the first valid entry, or before the empty entry 16 bytes earlier, are the vertex descriptor mask, 2 bits per attribute in the order matrix index, position, normal, colour 0, colour 1, UV 0, UV 1, UV 2 (1 and 2: byte index, 3: u16 index), which gives the exact vertex layout; the six u32 at sector + 0x1c are offsets (from sector + 0x1c) to the stream headers `{u32 data offset, u16 count, u16 type << 12 | shift << 8}` for position, colour, UV 0 and so on (colour type 2 is RGB plus a pad byte, type 5 RGBA; UV type 2 is u8 pairs, 3 is u16 pairs, 4 is f32 pairs, divided by 2^shift). Colours are written as `v x y z r g b` and UVs as `vt`; the heuristic UV search is only a fallback. Skin weights are in a block at record + 116 (u16 counts of 1-bone, 2-bone and n-bone groups, then three u32 offsets) whose groups name vertices by the raw 12 bytes of position and normal (1-bone groups 64 bytes, 2-bone 116 bytes with two byte weights per vertex, n-bone 68 bytes with a u16 vertex index and a byte weight each, weights /256); they are written to `<name>.skin.txt` together with each display list's bone and material (the weights of every vertex of Leo's body sum to 1). Layout derived from reading the community Manhunt 2 MDL Editor's Wii DFF to PC MDL converter. The bone hierarchy is written to `<name>.bones.txt` for character models: the u32 at 0x20 is the first model header `{u32 next (0x20 ends), ?, u32 bone block}`, the bone block starts with the root frame pointer, and a frame is `{u32 id, u32 next sibling, u32 parent, u32 root, u32 first child, u32 ?, char name[40], two 4x4 float matrices}` (the first is the local transform: rotation plus translation in the last row). A skin weight's bone number is the frame's index in that list for the model the mesh belongs to (checked on Leo: bone 16 is `Bip01_Spine2`, 20 the left clavicle); the model and the object name of each mesh are found through the ring of object nodes at bone block + 20 (`{u32 next, ?, u32 frame, u32 mesh record}`) and are written as a `mesh model N name X record R` line in `<name>.skin.txt`. The bone block also holds a table (frame at +20, u32 count, entry and matrix pointers at +12 and +16 of that block) of `{s16 id, s16 flags, u32 frame}` per bone in the same order |

| **PMSH (phx)** | Switch 2 / Model | ✅ | ❌ | *Kirby Air Riders* meshes from the PARC catalog: `Main` flatbuffer (`.pmsh`: vertex streams {attribute, size, offset, format} and submeshes {material, vertex count, first index, index count}) over the `GBuf` block (`.pmsh.gbuf`) → GLB with one primitive per submesh: float3 positions, unorm 10:10:10:2 normals, half-float UV0, RGBA8 colour 0, u16/u32 indices.
